# CHRISTIAN HEALING

## FILLMORE <note>Charles Fillmore (1854–1948) was a prominent American mystic and a central figure in the New Thought movement. Along with his wife, Myrtle, he co-founded the Unity School of Christianity. This book, first published in 1909, serves as a foundational guide to his metaphysical teachings, focusing on the relationship between spiritual consciousness and physical health.</note>

# PREFACE

These are not simply lectures, but lessons to be applied just as one applies mathematical rules—they are not meant to be merely read, but studied. When a suggestion is made to "hold a thought," or to affirm or deny a certain proposition <note>original: "proposition"; here meaning a statement or spiritual principle</note>, the student should stop reading and, both out loud and mentally, do as they are told. This will create new thought-currents in the mind and body, making way for the spiritual illumination <note>original: "illumination"; refers to a state of sudden spiritual understanding or "enlightenment"</note> which will follow for all who are faithful.

The statements following each lesson should be used for mental discipline. Write these statements down and apply them daily while studying the lesson to which they correspond. Anyone can perform mind healing <note>original: "mind healing"; the practice of using focused thought and prayer to affect physical health</note> if they use the simple rules of denials and affirmations set forth here. 

If you wish to heal another person, keep them in your mind and mentally repeat the denials <note>the act of stating that a negative condition has no ultimate power</note> and affirmations <note>the act of asserting a positive spiritual truth</note>. This will raise your consciousness to Spiritual Reality, which is where all healing power begins. If you wish to heal yourself, speak to your own mind and body just as you would talk to a patient.

<summary>This preface introduces a practical guide to spiritual healing, emphasizing that the text should be treated as a workbook for mental and physical transformation rather than a passive reading experience. It introduces the core techniques of "denials" and "affirmations" as tools for disciplining the mind and accessing spiritual power.</summary>
<keywords>mind healing, denials, affirmations, spiritual illumination, mental discipline, consciousness, spiritual reality</keywords>

# LESSON ONE

## THE TRUE CHARACTER OF BEING

<image-desc>An ornamental drop cap 'T' within a square frame, decorated with intricate floral and scrollwork designs.</image-desc>

“There is a spirit in man, and the inspiration of the Almighty gives him understanding.” <note>original: Job 32:8</note> The Science presented here is founded upon Spirit; it is no less scientific simply because it does not conform to conventional intellectual standards. The facts of Spirit are spiritual in nature and, when understood in their proper relationship, are orderly—which is the ultimate test of true science.

The unchanging truths of Spirit are more scientific than constantly shifting intellectual standards. The only real science is the Science of Spirit <note>In this context, "Science" refers to a systematic study of spiritual laws rather than material biology or physics.</note>. It never changes. It is universally accepted by all who live in the Spirit, but it is necessary to be “in the Spirit” before it can be understood. The mind of the Spirit must become active in those who wish to grasp the orderly Science of Being that these lessons proclaim.

It is not strictly necessary for the spiritual part of a person's nature to be active at the beginning of this study. The primary goal of these lessons is to awaken <note>original: "quickened"</note> this realm of consciousness and bring about the “inspiration of the Almighty” that provides understanding.

So let it be understood that we are teaching the

<vocab>Science of Spirit, Science of Being, spiritual character, intellectual standards, consciousness, inspiration of the Almighty</vocab>

<summary>This page introduces the concept of the "Science of Spirit," arguing that spiritual truths are more consistent and orderly than intellectual ones. It establishes that the goal of the lessons is to awaken the reader's spiritual consciousness and understanding.</summary>
<keywords>Spirit, Science of Being, consciousness, spiritual law, metaphysics, divine inspiration</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the introduction to Lesson One, specifically focusing on the relationship between humanity and the "Almighty" through the lens of logic and spiritual law.</meta>

<page-num>6</page-num>
<header>LESSON ONE</header>

Science of Spirit, and that those who are receptive will be inspired toward spiritual consciousness. This inspiration of the Spirit mentioned in the Book of Job is not a difficult matter to understand. We are all inspired by the Spirit in certain states of consciousness. Understanding the laws governing the realm of Spirit will make it possible to reach this state of awareness and receive this inspiration whenever the necessary requirements are met.

The starting point for this realization is a correct understanding of the One designated as "the Almighty." It is strictly logical and scientific to assume that humanity originates from this One, who is called by many different names, but whom everyone agrees is the source of all things. Since humanity is the offspring of the Almighty, we must share the character of our Parent. If an earthly child resembles its parents, how much more should the heavenly child resemble its Creator? This truth disproves the common assumption that it is impossible for the finite <note>original: "finite"; here meaning limited human beings</note> to understand the Infinite <note>original: "Infinite"; the limitless nature of God</note>. God must be present in the universe as an omnipresent, intelligent power, or else the universe would fall apart. God exists within the universe as its constant inspiration; therefore, we only need to find the point of connection to understand the One in whom we all "live, move, and have our being" <note>A reference to Acts 17:28</note>.

Logic is a fundamental component of human nature, and all minds naturally agree with logical conclusions. We can all see the relationship and unity between cause and effect when stated as a mental concept. However, because the physical world <note>original: "realm of forms"</note> does not always seem to reflect our initial assumptions <note>original: "premise"</note>, we often abandon true standards and try to convince ourselves that our logic is somehow...

<summary>This page argues that humans, as "offspring" of the Almighty, possess a divine character that allows them to understand the Infinite through logic and spiritual laws. It presents God as an omnipresent, intelligent power that serves as the universe's constant inspiration and point of connection for humanity.</summary>
<keywords>Science of Spirit, spiritual consciousness, The Almighty, Infinite vs Finite, Cause and Effect, Logic, Omnipresence, New Thought</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the discrepancy between spiritual logic and physical appearances, using a mathematical analogy to explain why the physical world may seem "defective" despite having a perfect Creator.</meta>

<page-num>7</page-num>
<header>THE TRUE CHARACTER OF BEING</header>

...how defective. The one important thing the student of **spiritual science** <note>The study of the laws of the mind and spirit, similar to how one studies physical sciences.</note> must learn is to trust the logic of the mind. If appearances are out of harmony with your mental premise, do not let them unseat your logic.

> "Do not judge according to appearance, but judge with righteous judgment." <note>original: "Judge not according to the appearance, but judge righteous judgment"; a quote from John 7:24.</note>

You would not take the confused figures of a child working a problem in mathematics as an example of the truth of mathematical principles. Furthermore, you could not detect the error in that problem unless you were already somewhat familiar with the rules of mathematics. Mental propositions—the ideas we hold as true—are the standards and governing principles in every science used by humanity. In the science of creation, the same rule holds good. You may rest in the assurance that the principles you mentally perceive as true of <term>God</term> are inviolate <note>original: "inviolate"; never broken, infringed, or dishonored.</note>. If there seems to be an error in how these principles work out in the world, it is because of some misapplication on the part of the demonstrator <note>The individual practicing or trying to prove the spiritual principle.</note>. By holding to the principle and insisting upon its accuracy, you open the way to a fuller understanding of it and to the cause of the errors in the demonstration.

Then, if you have been in mental confusion while contemplating a good <term>God</term> and an evil world, and have consequently fallen into skeptical ways, the only true remedy is to stand by the pure reason of your <term>spiritual perception</term> and let it clear up the proposition for you. Dismiss all prejudices based upon confused perceptions, and make your mind receptive to the clearer understanding, which will surely appear when you have...

<summary>This page argues that students of spiritual science must trust logic over physical appearances, using the analogy of mathematics to show that human errors do not invalidate divine principles.</summary>
<keywords>spiritual science, logic, mathematics, spiritual perception, God, righteous judgment, demonstration, principle vs appearance</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the argument that spiritual logic must override physical appearances, specifically regarding the belief that the "Primal Cause" (God) is entirely good despite worldly errors.</meta>

<page-num>8</page-num>
<header>Lesson One</header>

...taken sides with the Spirit and looked to it alone to solve the problem.

This is not blind belief, but an acceptance of the logic of the <term>superconsciousness</term> <note>A higher state of mind or awareness that connects the individual to divine wisdom and intuition.</note> of your own being, which is the only reliable guide through the complexities of the creative process. It is by trusting this superconscious mind that we open ourselves to the inspiration of the **Almighty**. Spirituality can be cultivated, and the "deep things of God" can be revealed to anyone who will mentally proclaim and affirm the logical understanding of the goodness and truth of **Being**.

The central premise in the inspiration of the Spirit is that God, or the **Primal Cause**, is good. It does not make a great difference what you name this Primal Cause; the important consideration is having a correct concept of its character. The Hindu calls it **Brahman** <note>original: "Brahm"; in Hindu philosophy, the ultimate reality or supreme cosmic power.</note>, a being of such immense proportions that a person feels insignificant when contemplating it. Although this greatness of **Being** is true, there is also another point of view—the minuteness of that same Spirit, as shown by the presence of its life in even the most insignificant creations. 

So, in order to get to the very heart of Being, it is necessary to realize that it manifests in the smallest things as well as in the greatest, and that in the creation of a universe, not one single idea could be removed without unbalancing the whole. This leads us to a fuller realization of our own importance in the universe and the necessity of finding our rightful place. It also places us

<summary>This page argues that God, as the Primal Cause, is inherently good and present in every part of creation, from the vast to the minute. It encourages readers to trust their "superconsciousness" to find their unique and essential place within the balance of the universe.</summary>

<keywords>Spirit, superconsciousness, Primal Cause, Brahman, Divine Goodness, inherent value, spiritual logic</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the nature of Being, shifting from the idea of God’s presence in even the smallest things to the concept of God’s immanence—meaning His presence within the human spirit and the natural world.</meta>

<page-num>9</page-num>
<header>THE TRUE CHARACTER OF BEING</header>

...in very close touch with the Father of all, the one omnipresent intelligence pervading everything.

The Father within you, so lovingly and familiarly revealed by Jesus, is not far away in a place called "heaven," but His abode is in the spiritual realms which underlie all creative forces. As Jesus realized and taught, "The kingdom of God is within you." On the invisible side of human nature is the home of the Spirit, which is the seat of power.

This revelation of God immanent <note>Existing or remaining within; the idea that God is present throughout the universe.</note> in the universe was clearly set forth by Paul: "In you all, through you all, and above you all." The inspired ministers of today are proclaiming the same. Reverend Doctor Lyman Abbott <note>A prominent American congregationalist theologian and editor (1835–1922) known for reconciling Christian theology with evolution.</note> once said:

> "The conception of God as a first great cause, who ages ago set in motion certain secondary causes which control the world, and with which He interferes from time to time as exigency <note>urgent necessity or emergency</note> may require, is giving place to a conception of one great, eternal, underlying cause, as truly active today as He was in the days of old. This energy is an intelligent energy. The relations of the physical world are intellectual relations. Science does not create them; it discovers them. Science thinks the thoughts of God after Him.
>
> "While science has thus been leading us to see God in physical nature, philosophy has been leading us to see God in all the events of history. The doctrine of evolution, which is not the same as Darwinism <note>Abbott distinguishes between the general concept of progress/development and Charles Darwin's specific biological theory of natural selection.</note>, is the doctrine that the world’s progress is from a lower to a higher stage, from a simple to a more complex condition. Thus, history is no longer the mere record of great events, or the story of great lives; it is the philosophical unfolding of a great development, the goal and result of which is the kingdom of God on earth."

<summary>This page explores the concept of God as an immanent, intelligent energy residing within humanity and nature, rather than a distant creator. It uses the perspectives of Jesus, Paul, and theologian Lyman Abbott to argue that science and history are the unfolding of God’s divine plan and thoughts.</summary>

<keywords>immanence, Kingdom of God, Lyman Abbott, evolution vs. Darwinism, Primal Cause, omnipresent intelligence, spiritual realms, theology</keywords>

<header>LESSON ONE</header>

> "But does this God have any relationship to the individual, so that we can have some consciousness of Him and some connection with Him? Or is there an invisible curtain between the soul and this power in nature—this righteous direction of history? Literature is the interpreter of life, and we turn to literature for an answer to this question. This answer is given to us by the great poets, and by no one more clearly and beautifully than by Tennyson:
>
> ->"Speak to Him, for He hears, and spirit can meet with spirit;<-
> ->He is closer than breathing, and nearer than hands or feet." <note>These lines are from Alfred Lord Tennyson’s poem "The Higher Pantheism" (1869), which suggests that the physical world is a manifestation of God, who is immediately accessible to the human spirit.</note><-
>
> "The notion of a human-like <note>original: "humanized"</note> God sitting at the center of the universe ruling things is gone. In its place, science has brought us back to this: 'We are always in the presence of the Infinite'; and history has brought us back to this: 'There is a power, not ourselves, that makes for righteousness'; <note>This famous definition of God was coined by the Victorian poet and critic Matthew Arnold in his book *Literature and Dogma* (1873).</note> and literature has brought us back to this: 'Spirit can meet with spirit. He is closer than breathing, and nearer than hands or feet.' Would you think that personality <note>In this context, "personality" refers to the quality of being a conscious, self-aware, and relating Being, rather than a cold, mechanical force.</note> was gone just because it was operating right before your eyes?
>
> "What we mean by personality is this: the infinite and eternal energy, from which all things proceed, is an energy that thinks, feels, intends, and acts. It is thinking, feeling, intending, and acting as a conscious life, of which our own is only a poor and broken reflection. I think we have stood at the foot of the cross or at the door of the tomb for too long and have not seen the stone rolled away and the triumphant Savior emerging. We have thought of the life of Christ ending with His passion <note>Archaic use referring to the suffering and crucifixion of Jesus.</note> and death for too long. But the greater part of His life is His life after the resurrection."

This fuller understanding of the very near presence of the creating and sustaining power of the universe, including humanity, has awakened a great inquiry into the character of this all-pervading One, and on every hand...

<header>THE TRUE CHARACTER OF BEING</header>

People are earnestly seeking to know God and to enter into a harmonious relationship with Him. Some are succeeding, while others seem to make very little progress. These different results are caused by the various ways of approaching the **One Mind**—for that is what God is. The mind is the key to this entire situation, and when humanity clearly understands the **Science of Mind** <note>The "Science of Mind" refers to a spiritual philosophy (often associated with the New Thought movement) that suggests our thoughts and mental states directly influence our physical reality and our connection to the Divine.</note>, we will easily solve all the mysteries of creation.

The dictionary definitions of "mind" and "spirit" are almost identical; using this comparison, we can more easily connect with God. If spirit and mind mean the same thing, we quickly realize that there is no great mystery surrounding spiritual matters, nor are they distant from our daily thoughts and experiences. "You are the temple of God, and His spirit dwells in you" <note>original: "Ye are the temple of God, and his spirit dwelleth in you." A reference to the New Testament, 1 Corinthians 3:16.</note> simply means that God lives within us in the same way our minds live within our bodies. Therefore, we see that God creates and directs all of creation through the power of mind, using thoughts as His instruments <note>original: "vehicles"</note>. It is through our own minds that we shall find God and do His will.

Mental laws are being discovered, observed, and recorded by researchers today more than ever before in history. Humanity is capable enough to see the different factors involved in the mind's creative processes. Through the study of these mental laws, we are beginning to recognize and accept the systematic principles <term>science</term> behind ideas, thoughts, and words. However, those who investigate **Nature** and her laws solely from an intellectual or physical perspective fail to reach a complete understanding, because they...

It suggests that understanding "mental laws" is the key to unlocking the mysteries of creation.</summary>

<keywords>One Mind, Science of Mind, Spirit vs Mind, Indwelling God, Mental Laws, New Thought philosophy, Creative process</keywords>

...fail to trace back to the Causing Mind the many symbols that make up the visible universe. The material forms we see around us are like the chalk marks of a great problem being solved by the One Mind. To understand that problem and catch even a small glimpse of its meaning, we must grasp the ideas that these chalk marks represent. This is what we mean by studying the Mind behind Nature. Humanity is mind, and we are capable of understanding the plan and specific ideas of the Supreme Mind.

Divine Ideas are humanity's inheritance—ideas full of every possibility, because ideas are the foundation and cause of everything that we desire.

With this understanding as a foundation, we easily perceive how "all that is mine is yours" <note>original: "all mine are thine." This is a reference to John 17:10, suggesting that the resources of the Divine Mind are fully available to the individual.</note>. All the ideas contained within the One Father Mind are at the mental command of every child of that Mind. Go behind the physical object into the mental realm where it exists as an inexhaustible idea; then you can draw upon it forever and never empty the source.

With this understanding of the potential of the Primal Cause <note>The "Primal Cause" refers to the original source or first principle of all existence, identified here as God or the One Mind.</note>, we find it a simple matter to solve the problem of life—the key to the situation being *ideas*. Thus, life as it is expressed is activity; in its essential Being, it is an *idea* of activity. To make life appear in the visible world, we only need to open our minds and thoughts to the Divine Idea of life and activity, and behold! all visible reality obeys us. It is through this understanding, and its practice in various degrees, that people have acquired...

# THE TRUE CHARACTER OF BEING

...the ability to raise dead bodies. Jesus understood this Realm of Supreme Ideas—or, as he termed it, "the kingdom of God within" (or among you)—and when he raised Lazarus, he called upon its power. When Martha spoke about a future resurrection, he said, "I am the resurrection and the life; he who believes in me, though he were dead, yet shall he live." One who identifies their whole mind with this Ever-Present <note>original: "Omnipresent"</note> Mind becomes so unified <note>original: "at-one"</note> with it that they can overcome death.

The reality of the universe is held in the Mind of Being as ideas of life, love, substance, intelligence, truth, and so on. These ideas may be combined in a multitude of ways, producing an infinite variety in the realm of physical forms. There is a right combination, which constitutes the Divine Order—the kingdom of heaven on earth. This correct relationship of ideas, and the science of right thinking, will form an important part of the future lessons of this course.

Students of the Science of Being should start all their investigations and mental processes from this foundation of the One Mind. If you are skeptical about the existence of God, or are an abstract believer without having had any personal experience or conscious mental awakening that has given you proof, you should be very diligent in prayer, affirmation, and invocation <note>The act of calling upon or appealing to a higher power for assistance or presence</note>. Remember, God is not a king who forces his presence upon you whether you wish it or not, but an Ever-Present Mind enfolding and flowing through <note>original: "interpenetrating"</note> all things.

There is goodness everlasting, and joy beyond ex—

<header>LESSON ONE</header>

...expression in a perfect union between your mind and this Perfect Mind. The point of contact is your own willingness and your search for it.

> “Seek, and you will find; knock, and it will be opened to you.” <note>Matthew 7:7</note>

The question naturally arises: if we are the offspring of this Divine Mind, why are we not naturally aware of its presence? The answer is that we have used our inherited privilege—the power to turn ideas into visible things—to create a realm that separates our consciousness from the Father Mind. This is what Jesus taught in the parable of the prodigal son <note>Luke 15:11-32; a story about a son who leaves his father's house, squanders his inheritance, and eventually returns home to a loving welcome</note>. When we grow tired of focusing only on what our physical senses perceive <note>original: "sense consciousness"</note>, we only need to turn our attention <note>original: "face (intelligence)"</note> toward our parent’s house to receive a loving welcome.

Understanding that God is not in a distant heaven, nor located in any specific geographical place, gives us a feeling of closeness and unity with the Parent Mind. Jesus beautifully demonstrated this inner communication between human consciousness and the Ever-Present <note>original: "Omnipresent"</note> Spiritual Force of the universe. God was closer to him than his own hands or feet <note>A reference to Alfred Lord Tennyson's poem "The Higher Pantheism": "Closer is He than breathing, and nearer than hands and feet."</note>. He attributed everything to this loving Father, who was in constant communication and cooperation with the Son. 

Yet even in his case, there was an independent personal consciousness that troubled him when he tried to be free from human <note>original: "mortal"</note> limitations. Therefore, we should not be discouraged or saddened if we do not quickly find the kingdom of God within ourselves. Jesus spent entire nights in prayer; we should not grow tired after just a few moments each day. A daily half-hour of meditation...

<page-num>15</page-num>
# THE TRUE CHARACTER OF BEING

...will open up the mind to a consciousness of the Inner One <note>A term for the divine spark or true spiritual self within a person</note>, and reveal many things that are hidden from the natural man <note>A biblical reference (1 Corinthians 2:14) to a person who understands the world only through physical senses rather than spiritual insight</note>.

The fact is, Truth cannot be imparted—it must be individually experienced. The presence of the Divine Mind in the soul cannot be told in words; it can be hinted at and referred to in parables, and compared to this or that, but never described exactly as it is. This ability of the individual mind to combine the ideas of Divine Mind into its own consciousness makes each of us the "only begotten son" <note>John 3:16; used here to emphasize that every person is a unique and direct expression of God</note>, a particular and special creation. No two individuals in all the universe are exactly alike, because there is always diversity in the ideas taken from the Divine Mind.

The truth is, then, that God is Principle, Law, Being, Mind, Spirit, All-Good, All-Powerful <note>original: "Omnipotent"</note>, All-Knowing <note>original: "Omniscient"</note>, Everywhere-Present <note>original: "Omnipresent"</note>, Unchangeable, Creator, Father, Cause, and Source of all that is.

God is individually formed in the consciousness of each of us and is known to us as "Father" when we recognize Him within us as our Creator, as our mind, as our life, and as our very being.

Mind has Ideas; Ideas have Expression. All manifestation <note>The physical appearance or visible result of a thought or spiritual reality</note> in our world is the result of the ideas we are holding in our minds and expressing.

To bring forth or manifest the harmony of the Divine Mind, or the "kingdom of heaven," all our ideas must be one with Divine Ideas and must be expressed in the Divine Order of that Mind.

## MANIFESTATION

<image-desc>An ornamental drop cap 'A' decorated with intricate floral and vine patterns within a square border.</image-desc>As a rule, religious people are not scientific. They think religion and science are separated by a wide gap and that the scientific mind is dangerous. To them, science is associated with Charles Darwin, Thomas Huxley, and other students of natural law who were skeptical about the scientific accuracy of the Bible. Because of their skepticism, these men were branded as infidels <note>original: "infidels"; a historical term used to describe people who did not believe in a specific religion or who doubted traditional religious doctrines.</note>. Consequently, many good Christians have come to view it as almost heresy to think of their religion as having a scientific side.

By science, we mean the systematic and orderly arrangement of knowledge. This definition does not limit science only to the material world. There is a science within Christianity, and it can only be fully proven in a person’s life by understanding this science as its foundation. To fail to understand the science upon which the Spirit rests is to fail in nearly every demonstration <note>original: "demonstration"; in this context, it refers to the practical proof or physical manifestation of spiritual laws.</note> of its power. The Apostle Paul says in 1 Corinthians 14:15, "I will pray with the Spirit, and I will pray with the understanding also."

There is a gap between the high spiritual realm and the most external material manifestation. Only by bridging this gap can science and religion be rec- <note>The word continues on the next page as "reconciled."</note>

reconciled. This bridge is the structure that thought builds. When Christians understand the science of thinking—how thought has the power to express itself and the process by which that expression happens—they will no longer fear material science. Likewise, when material scientists have grasped the true nature of that living force which they see as constantly active in all the structures of nature, they will have more respect for religion.

Both the religious believer and the physicist hold the view that the Bible is a historical description of the creation of humanity; however, neither is correct. Beginning with the very first chapter of Genesis, the Bible is an allegory <note>A story or description where the characters and events symbolize deeper moral or spiritual meanings.</note>. It is viewed this way by the majority of Hebrew scholars, and they certainly should understand the character of their own scriptures. The Apostle Paul was a Hebrew and was thoroughly well-versed in the esoteric <note>original: "occultism"; here referring to the study of hidden spiritual meanings or symbolic truths rather than modern supernatural practices.</note> nature of spiritual writings; referring to the story of Abraham and Sarah, he said, "These things are an allegory" <note>This refers to Galatians 4:24 in the New Testament.</note>. Jewish scholars almost universally claim that the story of the Garden of Eden, Adam and Eve, and the serpent is symbolism.

In light of these facts, it seems strange that traditional Christianity should insist on treating the Bible as literal history. This way of looking at it is what has stifled true spiritual understanding. When read through the lens of the Spirit, the first chapter of Genesis is a symbolic description of the creative action of the Universal Mind <note>A term used to describe the infinite, creative intelligence of God that exists as the source of all ideas.</note> in the realm of ideas. It does not refer to the physical universe any more than an inventor’s initial idea refers to the physical machine they later build.

First, a problem is thought out, and afterward, the structure is produced. This is how God builds his universe. This process is explained in the second chapter of Genesis, which says that God “rested from his work,” and yet there were no plants in the field, “and there was not a man to till the ground.” “And the Lord God formed man of the dust of the ground, and breathed into his nostrils the breath of life; and man became a living soul.”

We can only understand and reconcile these two apparently contradictory chapters through the perception of the mental law by which ideas manifest from the formless to the formed. But in the light of this understanding, everything is made plain. We discern exactly how the Divine Mind <note>A term often used in metaphysical Christianity to describe God as the source of all intelligence and the origin of all ideas.</note> is creating humanity and the universe: first the ideal concept, then the manifestation.

The six days’ creation, as described in the first chapter of Genesis, represents six great ideal projections from Divine Mind. Each is more comprehensive than its predecessor, until the final climax is reached in the sixth degree. At this point, the declaration is that a being, described as man, shall appear who shall have dominion <term>authority or sovereignty</term> over everything—or every idea—that has gone before. 

This ideal man, who is the “image and likeness” of God <note>original: "Elohim"; a Hebrew name for God used in the first chapter of Genesis, often interpreted in a metaphysical context as the plural majesty of Divine Mind's creative powers.</note>, is the epitome and focal center around which all creation revolves. # LESSON FOUR

## THE FORMATIVE POWER OF THOUGHT

It is universally accepted that the body is moved by thought, but it is not so widely admitted that thought is also the builder of the body. We know that thought moves the various parts <note>original: "members"</note> of the body because we constantly observe the close connection <note>original: "sympathy"</note> between thought and action. Before I run, I think about running, and in my imagination, my legs begin to move swiftly before I even start the physical action. 

Experiments conducted at Harvard College <note>This likely refers to early psychological research in the late 19th century, such as that by William James or Angelo Mosso, whose "human circulation balance" measured blood flow redistribution during mental activity.</note> have shown that the mere thought of running directs blood flow to the legs. A person is placed flat on their back on a balanced beam, which is adjusted so that the slightest weight at the head or foot will be recorded on an indicator. When a perfect balance is achieved, the person is given a math problem to solve. Immediately, the indicator shows an increase in weight at the head, showing that the thought process has called blood there. 

Then, the person is told to imagine that they are running, and again the indicator shows the weight gradually shifting toward the feet. This device is so finely <note>original: "nicely"</note> adjusted that it reveals the exact weight of the substance <note>In this context, "substance" refers specifically to the physical blood moving within the body.</note> that shifts its position according to the specific thoughts being held.

Here is proof that thought not only moves the ex— <note>The text cuts off mid-word, likely continuing with "external" or "exterior" on the following page.</note>

<summary>This lesson argues that thought is not just a driver of movement but a physical builder of the body, citing Harvard experiments where blood flow shifted based on a subject's mental focus.</summary>
<keywords>formative power of thought, mind-body connection, Harvard College experiments, physiology, mental influence, balanced beam experiment</keywords>

--- Page 44 ---
<meta>This page continues the argument that thought is the primary formative power of the body, moving from the physical circulation of blood to more subtle biological forces and the concept of "Divine Nature."</meta>

# LESSON FOUR

---

external parts <note>original: "members"</note> of the body, but also the flowing fluids within it. If thought moves the blood so easily from place to place, who can say that it does not move the <term>nerve fluid</term> even more quickly, or that even more volatile substance, the <term>magnetic force</term> that flows through every living thing? We claim that it does, and that thought not only moves these various elements of the body, but that it actually creates them as well.

<note>The "nerve fluid" and "magnetic force" mentioned here reflect 19th-century "vitalist" theories. Before modern neurology, it was common to believe that a physical fluid or an invisible magnetic energy carried signals through the nerves and animated the body.</note>

Respected medical authorities tell us that certain organs of the body are self-renewing, and they find it a puzzle how these organs ever wear out. If you owned a sewing machine that had the power to constantly replace the tiny particles worn away by friction, could you imagine a time when that machine would ever be destroyed? When healthy, a human body has this very power, and when it is in harmony, it never wears out. The harmony referred to here is an alignment with the Law of Being, the Law of Divine Nature, or the Law of God. It does not matter what you call this fundamental Principle underlying all life—the important thing is to understand it and bring yourself into harmony with it.

We have always been told that we would be healthy if we followed the laws of Nature, but no one has been able to tell us exactly what those laws are. Some have said that this conformity consists in eating the right kind of food, drinking the right kind of water in the right way, breathing pure air, or wearing suitable clothes. We have done all these things, and yet something is still lacking. # LESSON FOUR

A human being, then, is the key to God and the universe, and he may know all things by studying his own nature <note>original: "constitution"</note>. Supreme in this makeup is the mind. We must base all our research upon the mind, because it is the starting point of every thought, act, and form.

Some metaphysicians teach that humans make themselves; others teach that God makes them; and others teach that the creative process is a cooperation between God and humanity. > “My Father is working even until now, and I am working.” — (John 5:17)

Thought is humanity's creative power. ## HOW TO CONTROL THOUGHT

<image-desc>Ornamental initial 'T' featuring swirling vine and leaf motifs.</image-desc>The thoughts of the mind are identities that have a central ego. Thoughts are capable of expressing themselves—they think. Humanity thinks, and we think into our thoughts all that we are; therefore, those thoughts must be endowed with a secondary power of thought.

There is, however, a difference between the original thinker and the secondary thought. One has its animating center in Spirit, and the other in thought. One is the Son of God, and the other the Son of man. <note>original: "Son of God... Son of man." In this metaphysical context, "Son of God" usually refers to the divine or spiritual identity of a person, while "Son of man" refers to the human, mental, or physical identity.</note>

Every form in the universe—every function, all action, and all substance—has a thinking part that is receptive to and can be controlled by humanity. Thomas Edison <note>Thomas Alva Edison (1847–1931), the famous American inventor, who occasionally expressed philosophical views regarding the inherent intelligence of atoms and molecules.</note> said he observed that every molecule has three things: intelligence, substance, and action. It knows where it wants to go, it has form, and it moves.

For example, a businessman who has been focused for years on making money, but who then suffers a large loss and mourns over it, will likely develop some kind of kidney trouble. The process of breaking down or decaying; the loss of structural integrity.</note>. 

Physicians have marveled that so many public figures suffer from <term>diabetes</term> and <term>Bright's disease</term> <note>original: "Bright's disease." # LESSON SIX

## THE WORD

<image-desc>Ornamental decorative drop cap letter "I" at the start of the first paragraph, featuring intertwining floral or vine patterns within a square frame.</image-desc>
In pure metaphysics, there is only one Word: the Word of God. This is the original Creative Word, or the thought of Being. It is the "God said" described in the book of Genesis. It is also referred to in the first chapter of the Gospel of John as the *Logos* <note>original: Logos; a Greek term meaning "word," "reason," or "plan," used here to describe the divine intellect in action</note>. This term cannot be adequately translated into English. In the original Greek, it encompasses wisdom, judgment, power, and, in fact, all the inherent potential within Being. This Divine *Logos* was, and always is, in God; in fact, it is God acting as Creative Power. The Divine Mind creates according to law; specifically, mental law. We can understand the creative process of Being by analyzing the activity of our own minds. First comes the mind, then the idea within the mind of what the action will be, and finally the action itself. Within the Divine Mind, this idea is called the Word.

According to Genesis and all mystical writings regarding creation, the Divine Mind expresses its Word, and through the activity of that Word, the universe is brought into existence. Humanity is the ultimate fulfillment <note>original: consummation</note> of the Word, and the human spirit contains the concentrated essence of everything within that Word. Jesus is called the Word of God. That Word became flesh and lived among <note>This sentence ends abruptly as it continues onto the next page, referencing John 1:14: "And the Word was made flesh, and dwelt among us."</note>

<vocab>metaphysics, Word of God, Creative Word, Being, Genesis, Logos, Divine Mind, mental law, Universe, Jesus</vocab>

<summary>This page introduces the concept of "The Word" as the fundamental creative power of God, identifying it with the Greek term Logos. It explains that creation follows a mental law—moving from mind to idea to action—and asserts that humanity is the highest expression of this divine process.</summary>

<keywords>The Word, Logos, Divine Mind, Creative Word, metaphysics, creation law, Jesus Christ, Genesis, mental process</keywords>

<page-num>61</page-num>

# THE WORD

...us, and we beheld his glory as the "only begotten of the Father." Since God is perfect, His idea, thought, or Word must also be perfect, and this is the **spiritual man**. It is through this "man," or the Word of God, that all things are brought into manifestation <note>original: manifestation; the act of becoming visible or real in the physical world</note>, or made. "And without the Word, nothing was made that has been made." That Word is the "only begotten of God" because there is only one idea of humanity in the Divine Mind, and that is the perfect pattern for a human being.

In the first chapter of the Gospel of John, it is implied that some things are made that do not follow Divine Ideals; consequently, these things are not real. The creations of the Word of God are permanent and incorruptible. As an imitator of the Divine Mind, a person has the power to form and bring into reality whatever they imagine. However, unless their thought is united with the Divine Mind and guided in its actions by <term>Infinite Wisdom</term> <note>original: Infinite Wisdom; the perfect, all-knowing intelligence of God</note>, their creations are perishable and temporary.

Mental processes enter into all of creation. Physical science has discovered that every atom possesses substance, force, and intelligence—which are the essential building blocks of the mind. Mind is the one and only creative power, and all attempts to explain creation from any other perspective are useless. The creative processes of the mind are always active—creation is happening all the time—but the original plan or design within the <term>Divine Mind</term> is already finished.

A person cannot know how thought, or the Word, works except through their own consciousness. Therefore, one must understand, control, and organize their own word, because it is through their own word that they understand the <term>Word of God</term>.

# LESSON SIX

Our most important study, then, is our own consciousness. The ancient Greeks recognized this and inscribed over their temple, "Man, know thyself" <note>original Greek: Γνῶθι σεαυτόν (Gnothi seauton)</note>. The true Self of a human being is spiritual, and when it is in direct, conscious unity with the <term>Parent Mind</term> <note>original: Parent Mind; the source or creative intelligence of God</note>, it possesses real creative power. A person reflects their everyday thoughts, and their mind forms conditions that can even change the face of nature itself. Every thought that originates in the brain sends vibrations into the surrounding atmosphere, and all things are set into motion. The effect is proportional to the thinker's ability to concentrate their mental forces. Average thought vibrations produce only temporary results; however, under intense activity, conditions that are more or less permanent are impressed upon the sensitive plate <note>A metaphor comparing the mind's impact on the universe to how a camera's "sensitive plate" records a photographic image</note> of the <term>Universal Ether</term> <note>original: Universal Ether; in older science and metaphysics, a subtle substance believed to fill all space and transmit energy</note>, and through it, they are brought into physical manifestation.

Every idea originating in the <term>Divine Mind</term> is expressed in the mind of a person, and through their thoughts, it is brought to the outer plane of consciousness. Within the human organism itself are centers that respond to Divine Ideas, just as a musical instrument sympathetically responds to musical vibrations. Then, through another movement on what is called the conscious—or the most external—level of action, the thought is expressed as the spoken word. In the physical person, or the body, there is a point of concentration for this word, through which it is expressed in invisible vibrations. For example, at the root of the tongue is a brain center, and through it, the mind controls the larynx, tongue, and all the organs used in forming words.

# THE WORD

By following the law of creation from the formless to the formed, we can see how an idea fundamental to the **Divine Mind** is reflected into the human mind, takes shape in our thoughts, and is expressed through our spoken words. If we aligned with the **Divine Creative Law** at every step of this process, our words would create things instantly, just as Jesus created the loaves and fishes. However, because we have partially lost the steps in this creative process—the movement from the internal to the external—there are many disruptions and unnatural conditions, resulting in more failures than successes in what we produce.

Nevertheless, every word has an effect, even if it is unseen or unrecognized. Jesus said that a person should be held accountable for "every idle word" <note>Matthew 12:36: "But I say unto you, That every idle word that men shall speak, they shall give account thereof in the day of judgment."</note>, and a close observation of how the mind influences an individual’s circumstances proves this is true. What we think, we usually express in words, and those words bring about in our lives and affairs whatever meaning we put into them. A weak thought in the mind is followed by words of weakness; through the law of expression and form, these words change the character of everything that receives them.

The nerves are like wires that transmit messages to every part of the body. As **thought formations** <note>original: thought formations; the metaphysical concept that physical structures like nerves are the outward manifestation or "solidified" version of mental energy</note>, the nerves in turn carry out the "word" that has been spoken into them. To talk about nervousness and weakness is to create those conditions in the body. On the other hand, to speak and send forth the word of strength and affirm **nerve poise** <note>original: nerve poise; a state of calm, emotional stability, and physical balance</note> will produce that result. To talk about a weak stomach will make your...

This amazing discovery that messages can be sent around the world without visible wires should permanently silence those who have been skeptical when people claim that thoughts can be transferred through a similar medium. In its consciousness, there is no distance or separation, and whoever places themselves in that state of consciousness can achieve results instantly.

When the centurion <note>original: centurion; a Roman military officer who, in the Gospel of Matthew, demonstrates great faith by believing Jesus can heal from a distance</note> said to Jesus, "Only speak the word, and my servant shall be healed," the Master said he had not found such great faith in all of Israel. [igno]rance and a lack of faith prevent us from realizing this truth. We should not only have faith in the **All-Presence** <note>All-Presence: the metaphysical concept that God or the Divine is present everywhere at all times</note>, but we should also develop our understanding so that we may know why this is true. Today, physical science is ahead of religion in its recognition of a universal life, substance, and intelligence. Religion looks for this mighty Creator in some distant heaven, despite the clear teaching of **Jesus Christ** that "God is Spirit" and His kingdom is within man.

But physical science also falls short because it fails to recognize the unity between this omnipresent Intelligence and the conscious principle within humanity. It seeks to understand through the intellect—or from the level of physical forms and shapes—that which actually belongs to the mind. **Physical science** has proven the presence of creative forces but does not know the power that moves them. **Divine Metaphysics** <term>Divine Metaphysics: a spiritual philosophy that explores the fundamental nature of reality and the relationship between human thought and the divine</term> has discovered that this moving power is the thought and **spoken word** of man, and it is proving this truth by producing results in a multitude of ways.

The spoken word carries vibrations through the **universal ether** <note>original: universal ether; a 19th-century scientific theory of an invisible substance filling all space, used here to explain how thoughts and words exert influence at a distance</note> and also moves the intelligence found in every form, whether living or non-living. It has only very recently been discovered that even rocks and all minerals have life. This serves as proof of the omnipresence of the **One Animating Substance**. Man, being the highest expression of the **Divine Mind**, has great power to direct these forces and is really...

The all-penetrating ether <note>original: ether; in late 19th-century science, this was a hypothetical substance believed to occupy all space. There are no secrets and no concealments. What you think and speak in the inner chamber is proclaimed from the rooftops, as Jesus said, and now we know why this is so. Not long ago, a lady rented a room in a certain city. For several nights in a row, just as she fell asleep, she heard a man talking incoherently about the grain market. This continued for some time, and she mentioned it to the landlady, who informed her that the room had been last occupied by a dealer on the board of trade <note>original: board of trade; a central marketplace where merchants trade commodities like grain</note>.

This Power of the Word is given to humanity to use. The better a person understands the character of God and their relation to humanity, the more unselfishly they will exercise this power. Just because some are using it in selfish ways should not deter others, who have a better understanding of the law, from using it in righteous ways. “Ask whatever you will in my name, and it shall be done for you” <note>original: “Ask whatsoever ye will in my name and it shall be done unto you,” quoting John 15:7</note>.

# LESSON SEVEN

## SPIRITUALITY; OR PRAYER AND PRAISE

<image-desc>An ornamental drop cap 'T' set within a square frame featuring a decorative floral and vine motif.</image-desc>
The Bible describes humanity in its wholeness—Spirit, soul, and body—through a multitude of symbols. These symbols include people, places, tents, temples, and more. The name of every person mentioned in the Bible carries a meaning that represents that person’s character. The twelve sons of Jacob represent the twelve foundational faculties <note>In this context, "faculties" refers to inherent mental or spiritual powers or senses</note> of a human being. When correctly interpreted, the name of each of these sons reveals the development and function <note>original: office</note> of its particular faculty as a trinity; that is, its relationship to consciousness within the Spirit, the soul, and the body. For example, when the sons of Jacob were born, their mothers revealed the character of the faculty which they represented, as set forth in chapters 29 and 30 of Genesis.

Regarding the birth of Reuben, it is written: “Leah conceived and bore a son, and she called his name Reuben; for she said, 'Surely the Lord has looked upon my affliction.'” The emphasis is placed upon the word “looked,” and by referring to a Concordance <note>A reference book that lists every word in the Bible and where it can be found, often including original Hebrew or Greek meanings</note>, we find that the meaning of the name Reuben is, “One who sees; vision of the sun.” It is clear that this refers to the bringing forth of sight.

# SPIRITUALITY; OR PRAYER AND PRAISE

> "And she conceived again, and bore a son; and said, 'Because the Lord has heard that I was hated.'"

Here the emphasis is upon the word "heard," and we find that the name Simeon means, "One who hears or obeys; that which is heard." This represents the bringing forth <note>The manifestation or birth of a spiritual quality</note> of the faculty of hearing.

> "And she conceived again, and bore a son; and said, 'Now this time will my husband be joined unto me.'"

In this case, the emphasis is upon the word "joined." Levi means unity, which in the body is expressed as feeling; in the soul, as sympathy; and in the Spirit, as love. Thus, each of these twelve faculties in the complete person functions in this threefold degree.

What is described here as the twelve sons of Jacob is the first, or natural, bringing forth of the faculties, which arrive at a higher expression in the twelve disciples of Jesus Christ. Simon Peter represents hearing and faith united. John represents feeling and love joined. When we believe what we hear, it forms in us the Substance of the Word, which is Peter: a rock, a sure foundation. 

> "Faith comes by hearing, and hearing by the Word of God." <note>A quotation from Romans 10:17</note>

The Bible is a very wonderful book, and as a person develops in spiritual understanding, it reveals itself to them; then they see why it has been reverenced and called holy by the people. It is a deep exposition of mental laws, and of the physiology of the body in its true estate—that is, mind in action, instead of mere material functions. But above all, the Bible explains the spiritual character of man and the laws governing his relation to God. This is all symbolically set forth as states of consciousness, illustrated by parables and allegories.

# SPIRITUALITY; OR PRAYER AND PRAISE

Spirituality follows the same law that draws people with similar ideas together into assemblies and communities. The head is the center of the intellectual man, the heart is the center of the emotional <note>original: "affectional"</note> man, and the abdomen is the center of the physical or sensual man. These areas are subdivided into many functions necessary to build up the manifested person as they are envisioned in the Divine Mind <note>In this context, the "Divine Mind" refers to the universal intelligence or the realm of perfect ideas from which all creation springs</note>.

At the very top <note>original: "apex"</note> of the brain is a nerve center <term>ganglionic center: a cluster of nerve cells that serves as a focal point for nerve impulses</term> which we may call Reverence or Spirituality. It is here that a person communicates with the intelligence of the Divine Mind. This center is the place, or "upper room," of spiritual consciousness, which the Bible identifies as Judah, and its duty is to pray and praise. This faculty is also the seat of action for that mysterious realm called the superconscious, which gives every thought an elevating, inspiring quality. All lofty ideals come from this faculty. It is the inspiration for everything that uplifts and idealizes religion, poetry, art, and in fact, all things that are true and real. Instead of being a humble plea for help, prayer should be a joyful expression of gratitude. It stimulates the mind far better than any medicine <note>original: "tonic"</note>, and like a powerful magnet, it attracts spiritual qualities that are then expressed in the mind, the body, and one’s daily affairs.

Spirituality is one of the fundamental powers <note>original: "foundation faculties"</note> of the mind. It is the consciousness that connects a person directly to the Father-Mind <note>The author uses this term to describe God as the source of all intelligence and creation</note>. It is energized and expanded through prayer and all forms of religious thought and worship. When we pray, we "look up" from within—not because God is far away in the sky, but because this spiritual...

Prayer is natural to humanity and should be cultivated in order to round out one's character. Prayer is the language of Spirituality; when developed, it makes a person master of the realm of creative ideas. In order to get results from using this faculty, right thinking should be practiced here as well as elsewhere. To pray while believing that the prayer may or may not be answered—depending on the whim or will of God—is missing the mark <note>original: "missing the mark"; this is a literal translation of the Greek word *hamartia*, often translated as "sin," suggesting an error in focus or alignment</note>. It is a law of mind that every idea is fulfilled as soon as it is conceived, and this law is true in Spirituality; therefore, we should see to it that we do as Jesus said: 

> "Pray believing that you have received, and you shall receive." 

In light of how the mind works <note>original: "mind action"</note>, this instruction is clear and absolutely necessary for an unfailing answer to prayer. If we pray asking for future fulfillment, we build that kind of thought-structure in our consciousness, and our prayers are always waiting for a future fulfillment that we have merely idealized. If we pray thinking that we do not deserve the things we ask for, these untrue and indefinite thoughts fulfill themselves, and we grow to look upon prayer with doubt and suspicion. This is called the "prayer of blind faith," but evidently it is not the kind that Jesus used, because his prayers were answered.

It should not be inferred that the will of the Divine Mind <term>Divine Mind: the universal source of all intelligence and perfect ideas</term> is to be set aside in prayer; we can pray that the will of God enter into us and become a moving factor.

<header>SPIRITUALITY; OR PRAYER AND PRAISE</header>

The Father does not take away our wills, but gives us the utmost freedom and the understanding of the law through which we can create any condition we desire. The promise, "Ask what you will in my name, and it shall be done for you," becomes our assurance.

One of the functions <note>original: "offices"</note> of Spirituality is to gather ideas. Through it, humanity can draw "God thoughts"—ideas that are absolutely true—from the Universal Mind <term>Universal Mind: the infinite, divine source of all intelligence and potential</term>. Thus, prayer is cumulative; it accumulates spiritual Substance <note>original: "Substance"; in this context, it refers to the underlying spiritual energy or "matter" from which all things are created</note>, Life, and Intelligence—everything necessary for our highest expression. When we pray with spiritual understanding, this highest realm of the mind comes into contact with the Universal and Impersonal Mind, and the very mind of God is joined to the mind of humanity. 

God answers our prayers through ideas, thoughts, and words, which are then translated into the external world across time and various conditions. It is therefore important that we pray with an understanding of the law, and always give thanks that our prayers have been answered and fulfilled, regardless of how things may appear on the surface. When Jesus multiplied the loaves and fishes, he prayed, blessed them, and gave thanks. With an understanding and realization of the relationship between an idea and its fulfillment, he overcame the slow processes of Nature, and the loaves and fishes increased rapidly. We may not be able to achieve this rapid use of the law immediately, but we will approach it and accelerate the process the more we hold our ideas to the perfection of the realm of ideas.

# SPIRITUALITY; OR PRAYER AND PRAISE

A linotype <term>linotype: a "line casting" machine used in printing that was common from the late 19th century until the 1970s; it was often temperamental and difficult to maintain</term> machine, which had been performing poorly, immediately began to work smoothly after being praised. A lady living in a country town had a rag carpet on her parlor floor that she had hoped for years might be replaced by a Brussels <term>Brussels carpet: a high-quality, looped-pile carpet known for its durability and intricate patterns</term> or an ingrain <term>ingrain carpet: a reversible, flat-weave carpet made of dyed yarn, common in middle-class homes in the 19th century</term> carpet. She heard of this law and began praising the old carpet. Within two weeks, greatly to her surprise, she was given a brand-new carpet from an unexpected source. These are a few simple illustrations of the possibilities hidden within the act of praise. Whether the physical objects were actually changed or the person's attitude toward them shifted does not matter, as long as the desired result was achieved.

Praise whatever you want to increase. Give thanks that it is now fulfilling your ideal. You can praise yourself from weakness into strength; from ignorance into intelligence; from poverty into wealth <note>original: "affluence"</note>; and from sickness into health. The young boy with a few loaves and fishes provided the "seed" that grew, through the prayer and thanksgiving of Jesus, until it was enough to feed five thousand people.

If we do not receive answers to our prayers, it is because we have not fully followed the spiritual law. "You ask and do not receive because you ask wrongly <note>original: "ask amiss." A reference to James 4:3, meaning to ask with the wrong motives or in a way that is not aligned with spiritual principles</note>." This does not mean that we ask the Lord for things we do not need, but rather that we miss the mark in *how* we ask—our relationship to the Divine Mind <term>Divine Mind: in New Thought philosophy, the infinite, creative intelligence of God that humans can access through their own thoughts</term> is not in harmony with the law. The failure is not in God, but in us. Therefore, we should never be discouraged but, like the prophet Elijah, persist until our prayers are answered.

Spirituality, faith, and love are God-given abilities <note>original: "faculties"</note>, and when we are raised in consciousness to their level <note>original: "plane"</note>, they naturally act under a spiritual law which we may not yet understand <note>original: "comprehend"</note>. There is a Law of Prayer, which humanity will eventually recognize and apply just as we now use the laws of mathematics or music.

Jesus said, "Ask whatever you desire in my name, and it shall be done for you." "In his name" means in his consciousness—that is, the spiritual or universal consciousness, instead of the personal. He attained a unity with the Divine Mind and realized that his thoughts and words were not from himself, but from God. When we pray in his name, we enter into his unity with the Father and share the same consciousness.

God is the ever-present, indwelling Mind. To realize God, we must quiet our outer thoughts and enter into the stillness, peace, and harmony of Spirit.

### FAITH

<image-desc>An ornamental drop cap letter "F" featuring a decorative border and a bird perched on a branch among leaves.</image-desc>> FAITH is the substance <note>original: "substance"; from the Greek 'hypostasis,' meaning a guaranteed foundation or a concrete reality.</note> of things hoped for; the evidence of things not seen. Through faith we understand that the worlds were framed <note>original: "framed"; meaning prepared, completed, or put in order.</note> by the Word of God, so that things which are seen were not made of things which do appear.—Hebrews 11:1.

In the 11th chapter of Hebrews, Paul piles the achievements of faith mountain high. “By faith Enoch was translated <note>original: "translated"; taken directly to heaven by God without experiencing physical death.</note> so that he should not see death. By faith Noah prepared an ark to the saving of his house. By faith Abraham, being tested, offered up Isaac. By faith Moses, when he was born, was hidden for three months by his parents. By faith the walls of Jericho fell down. And what more shall I say? For time would fail me if I told of Gideon, Barak, Samson, Jephthae; of David and Samuel and the prophets: who through faith subdued kingdoms, worked righteousness, obtained promises, stopped the mouths of lions, quenched the power of fire, escaped the edge of the sword, from weakness were made strong, became mighty <note>original: "waxed mighty"</note> in war, turned to flight the armies of foreigners <note>original: "aliens"; in this context, it refers to hostile foreign nations or invading armies.</note>, and women received their dead by a resurrection.”

The idea that faith is something that has to do only with one's religious experience is incorrect. Faith is a

That faith is a power is self-evident. People who have faith in themselves achieve far more than those who do not believe in their own ability. We call this innate confidence, but confidence is actually only a form of faith. 

Belief is another of faith’s expressions. Jesus apparently made no distinction between faith and belief. He said, “Do you believe <note>original: "Believe ye"</note> that I am able to do this?” and “Whoever shall not doubt in his heart, but shall believe that what he says comes to pass, he shall have it.” <note>These quotes refer to Matthew 9:28 and Mark 11:23.</note> In an analysis of the constituent parts of human consciousness, we locate belief in the intellect <note>original: "mentality"</note>, working in the realm of thought without contact with the more interior substance of the Spirit, upon which true faith is founded.

In the Spirit, faith is related to what Paul calls substance or assurance. Jesus Christ used the same illustration when he referred to Peter—a symbol <note>original: "type"</note> of faith—as a Rock upon which he founded his church. Faith must, then, be closely allied in Spirit to that which is considered substantial, enduring, firm, and unyielding in the world, with an added quality of power to act and bring about results in the affairs of those who cultivate it.

Like the other faculties, faith has a center through which it acts and expresses its spiritual powers outwardly. Physiologists call this center the pineal gland, and they locate it in the upper brain. By meditation, a person lights up the inner mind and knows more than can be

Intellectual people perform no miracles through faith because they always limit it to what the intellect defines as law. <header>FAITH</header>

Law <note>The author refers here to Divine Law or spiritual principles which, like physical laws, are thought to produce consistent results.</note> brings results that seem miraculous, without variation or disappointment.

Faith has always played a very large part in the experiences of religious people, because they have allowed it full expression <note>original: "free scope"</note> and expected great things from the Lord through it. However, nearly all faith demonstrations <note>In this context, a "demonstration" is the outward, physical manifestation of a spiritual belief or prayer.</note> have resulted from a kind of blind confidence that God would carry out whatever was asked of Him. Sometimes the person praying <note>original: "petitioner"</note> was disappointed, and a series of disappointments usually led to doubt and the conclusion that God had in some way changed His law. The early Christians were taught by Jesus and his disciples to have faith in God, and they performed wonderful and so-called miraculous works. As time passed and their attention was increasingly drawn to worldly things, the Christians of a later day became separated from their inner spiritual forces, and their faith lost its power <note>original: "energy"</note>. Then they began teaching that miracles were no longer necessary; they argued that God had granted them to the early Christians only because they did not yet have the Bible or an organized church. They also taught that miracles were given solely to prove that Jesus was the Son of God.

Now we have a fuller understanding of the law of God, and we know that whatever has been done can be done again under similar conditions. If Jesus, his disciples, and the early Christians performed marvelous things through the prayer of faith, we can do them too. All that is required is persistence in the use of faith until we make a connection with the higher realms of conscious-

### Lesson Eight

The development of the faith faculty <note>faith faculty: In New Thought and metaphysical Bible interpretation, a "faculty" is a specific power or "muscle" of the mind. Faith is seen here as a mental tool that can be strengthened through use.</note> in the mind is as necessary to the worker in spiritual principles as the development of the mathematical faculty is to the worker in mathematics. Neither of these faculties comes into consciousness fully formed at a single bound; rather, both grow through cultivation. "Increase our faith," said the disciples, and Jesus answered, "Have faith in God."

All readers of Scripture recognize Peter as a type <note>type: A symbolic figure or archetype representing a specific quality of the human mind or soul.</note> of faith, and we may, by studying his experiences, find hints regarding the development of that faculty in ourselves. Peter’s fluctuating allegiance to Jesus illustrates the growth of faith in someone who has had no previous development of that power. Faith and doubt contend for supremacy in Peter, and we wonder why Jesus should choose this vacillating, weak, and cowardly fisherman as his chief disciple. But we observe that Peter was enthusiastic and bold at times, and also receptive and patient under reproof. He had never walked on the water, but when Jesus said "come," he boldly went out to meet him. Doubt became dominant and he began to sink, but the helping hand was extended to him, and he was made stronger by the experience. This and many other illustrations in the history of Peter show how faith grows in the mind, and we should not be discouraged if our first efforts fall short of the desired end.

**A very little faith often produces surprising results**

The invisible forces are much closer than we think, and when we turn our attention in that direction, the response is usually so pronounced and so swift that we cannot...

<summary>This page compares the development of faith to the learning of mathematics, using the biblical figure of Peter to illustrate how faith grows through a mixture of boldness, failure, and persistence. It emphasizes that even small amounts of faith can connect a person to powerful, invisible spiritual forces.</summary>

<keywords>faith faculty, Apostle Peter, spiritual growth, New Thought, mental development, spiritual principles, doubt vs. faith, cultivation of the mind</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes a sentence from page 90 regarding the growth of faith and begins a discussion on the trio of spiritual faculties: Faith, Understanding, and Love.</meta>

<page-num>91</page-num>
<header>FAITH</header>

...but feel that a miracle has been performed. A closer understanding of the Divine Law <note>Divine Law: The spiritual principles that govern the universe, which the author suggests are as reliable as the laws of physics.</note> convinces us that all things are possible under it if we only believe and, at the same time, align our thoughts with its principles.

Peter (representing Faith), James (representing Understanding), and John (representing Love) were the three disciples closest to Jesus, and they are more prominent in his story than the others. This suggests that these three faculties <note>faculties: specific powers or "muscles" of the mind or soul.</note> develop before the others and are closely linked. Understanding shows us that God is a Mind Principle <note>Mind Principle: The metaphysical idea that God is the source of all intelligence and operates according to fixed mental laws.</note> founded upon ideas. When we realize our relationship with this Creative Principle, we see how easily we can communicate with God. Through this connection, we strengthen our faith almost without realizing it, finding that each faculty helps the others grow.

However, there must be room for this growth, and that space is created by Love. Selfishness acts as a limitation, trapping a person in a small prison called "personality" <note>personality: In this context, the "personality" refers to the limited, selfish ego, as opposed to the higher spiritual self.</note>. The only way to expand one's character and allow all the faculties to function is through love. Love expands our consciousness by removing thoughts of hatred and conflict. Be quick to make peace with all your adversaries <note>adversaries: anything that opposes progress, whether they are external people or internal negative thoughts.</note>, whether they are people, thoughts, or circumstances.

We are constantly creating the conditions of our lives through our thoughts. Some people claim that everything is working against them. If they miss a streetcar <note>car: original: "car." Referring to a trolley or streetcar, a common urban transport at the time of writing.</note>, they say, "That's just how it always goes." By building up that negative state of mind, everything begins to seem difficult and contrary to them.

<summary>This page explains that Faith, Understanding, and Love are the primary mental faculties that must be developed together to commune with God. It emphasizes that Love is the key to breaking free from the "prison" of the selfish personality and that our habitual thoughts create our daily reality.</summary>

<keywords>Faith, Understanding, Love, Divine Law, Mind Principle, Creative Principle, Peter, James, John, personality, consciousness, thought-power</keywords>

--- Page 96 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on spiritual growth, focusing on the practice of non-condemnation and the synergistic relationship between Faith, Understanding, and Love.</meta>

<page-num>92</page-num>
<header>LESSON EIGHT</header>

Throughout our lives, we should condemn nothing that happens to us and nothing that we do. We know the spiritual law <note>law: The author refers to "Divine Law" or "Mental Law," the idea that thoughts and actions produce equivalent results.</note>; let us follow it and avoid creating negative conditions through judgmental thoughts. Whatever you are doing, find happiness in it. If you are experiencing the wrong results, do not believe in an angry God. You are simply receiving the results of your own actions according to your level of faith. Be wise; judge nothing as evil, and only good will come of it. Should we call everything "good"? Yes. If a person in a primitive state <note>original: "savage"</note> knew this law, he could lift himself to a higher level of awareness <note>original: "consciousness"</note> by it. We escape ignorance <note>original: "savagery"</note> by recognizing the good.

Have faith in the natural <note>original: "innate"</note> goodness of all people and all circumstances. Do not condemn, no matter how great the provocation. Whatever you think, you create within your own mind. Expand your perspective, and you may see the good in what currently seems evil. God is good and God is everything; therefore, no *real* condition can exist except for the good. Why should we waste our time fighting against evil? If we build our character upon Faith, Understanding, and Love—with the great I AM <note>I AM: In metaphysical teaching, this refers to the divine spark or the presence of God within the individual.</note> as our central focus—we shall become pillars in the Temple of God.

<vocab>condemnation <note>the act of judging something as wrong or evil</note>, consciousness <note>awareness or the state of one's mind</note>, faith, innate goodness, I AM, Faith, Understanding, Love, Temple of God</vocab>

<summary>This page encourages readers to practice non-condemnation and recognize the inherent goodness in all things as a way to align with divine law. It emphasizes that our reality is shaped by our thoughts and that building character on faith, understanding, and love leads to spiritual strength.</summary>
<keywords>non-condemnation, spiritual law, innate goodness, I AM, Faith, Understanding, Love, higher consciousness</keywords>
