# FAITH

## FAITH AFFIRMATIONS

->(To be used in connection with Lesson Eight.)<-

> "Now faith is the reality <note>original: substance</note> of things hoped for, the evidence of things not seen."

Constantly holding onto the reality of spiritual things establishes them in the mind—they become a mental foundation <note>original: substance</note>.

I believe in the presence and power of the One Mind <note>One Mind: In metaphysical thought, this refers to the single, universal consciousness of God that everyone shares.</note>, and to me, it is the very foundation of intelligence <note>original: Substantial Intelligence</note>.

> "Let it be done for you according to your faith."

My doubts and fears are dissolved and cleared away, and I rest with confidence and peace in Your Unchangeable Law <note>Unchangeable Law: The spiritual principle that our thoughts and beliefs consistently create our life experiences.</note>.

> "Great is your faith; let it be done for you just as you desire."

With my mind's eye, I see more and more the reality of the True Ideas that always exist within the Divine Principle <note>Divine Principle: The fundamental spiritual truth or logic that underlies the universe.</note>.

> "I believe; help my unbelief!"

> Jesus said, "Have faith in God."

I am saved from pain and sorrow through my unwavering faith in the protection and care of God.

> "Lord, increase our faith."

My faith grows stronger day by day because it is planted in Truth, and through it, all the mountains of human mistakes <note>original: mortal error; the idea that suffering stems from a limited, physical perspective rather than spiritual truth</note> are moved into the sea of nothingness.

> "Your faith has made you well <note>original: whole</note>; go your way."

<vocab>
**Faith**: A deep trust and internal conviction in spiritual realities that are not yet visible.
**Affirmations**: Positive statements repeated to focus the mind and manifest spiritual truths in one's life.
**One Mind**: The concept of God as the universal intellect or consciousness.
**Substantial Intelligence**: The idea that divine wisdom is not abstract, but is a tangible "substance" that forms reality.
**Unchangeable Law**: The reliable, spiritual cause-and-effect relationship between thought and experience.
**Divine Principle**: The basic, unchanging nature of God as the source of all order.
**Truth**: The ultimate, spiritual reality of God's goodness, as opposed to temporary physical appearances.
**Mortal error**: The mistaken beliefs and fears of the limited human ego that lead to suffering.
</vocab>

It emphasizes that faith acts as a "mental substance" that transforms one's reality by aligning the individual mind with the "One Mind" of God.</summary>

<keywords>faith, affirmations, One Mind, Divine Principle, spiritual law, mental substance, healing, New Thought, metaphysics</keywords>

# LESSON NINE

## IMAGINATION

<image-desc>An ornamental drop cap 'T' set within a square frame decorated with intricate floral and vine patterns.</image-desc>The teaching regarding the things of the Spirit is often called mystical. The reason for this is that humanity has not yet become conscious of the many faculties <term>faculties: Inherent mental powers or abilities, such as reason, memory, or imagination.</term> necessary to comprehend the Spirit. Victor Hugo once said, “There are no occult or hidden truths; everything is luminous with mind.” Similarly, in the study of Truth, we find that what is labeled as mysterious or occult <note>occult: From the Latin *occultus*, meaning hidden or secret. In this context, it refers to spiritual laws that are not yet understood by the average person.</note> is simply a range of facts that humanity has not yet explored. When a person expands their mind and embraces a larger horizon, they see the relationship between a multitude of previously unknown laws which, from a limited viewpoint, seemed mysterious.

Mind expresses itself through faculties, and in order to understand on a larger scale, there must be an increase in these mental avenues. It is undeniable that we have latent <note>latent: Existing but not yet developed or manifest; hidden or dormant.</note> possibilities, and it is unthinkable that there should be any limit to the ability of the mind. Whatever a person imagines they can do, they can do. It is simply a matter of approaching it in the right way. However, allowing the imagination to drift in daydreams never brings anything to pass. Ideas must be worked up into living, breathing, thinking things. We can compress our wandering <note>original: vagrant</note> ideas into visibility...

<summary>This page introduces Lesson Nine on Imagination, arguing that what we call "mystical" or "occult" is simply unexplored mental territory. It emphasizes that the mind has unlimited potential and that the faculty of imagination must be actively directed to turn ideas into reality.</summary>

<keywords>imagination, mental faculties, Victor Hugo, occult, spiritual laws, latent possibilities, consciousness, Truth</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on Lesson Nine regarding the faculty of imagination. It concludes the analogy of the chemist compressing ideas into visible form and transitions into the relationship between the physical brain, the subconscious, and the universal Mind.</meta>

<page-num>95</page-num>

->IMAGINATION<-

ideas, just as the chemist liquefies and makes visible the intangible atmosphere; but to do this, he must, like that chemist, have the machinery.

Physiology <term>Physiology: The branch of biology that deals with the normal functions of living organisms and their parts.</term> says that to think, a person must have a brain. However, thinking is not limited to material cells; like everything in the universe, it has a wide range of expression. There are brains within brains and cells within cells. Throughout the body are brain centers whose function <note>original: office</note> has not yet been determined. Very recent discoveries in psychology show that these nerve centers are acted upon by invisible forces. Psychology teaches that humanity has what is called a subconscious mind that transcends the knowledge and ability of the conscious mind. A still higher teaching—that of Jesus Christ—is that humanity has a mind called "the Lord," which transcends both the conscious and the subconscious. Yet the harmonious working in unity of these seemingly three minds is necessary to bring forth a person's latent <note>latent: Existing but not yet developed or manifest; hidden or dormant.</note> possibilities.

In Truth, there is but one Mind, in which all things exist. To speak accurately, then, a person does not have three minds—nor even one mind—but instead expresses Mind in a multitude of ways. To believe that we possess a mind and that it is necessary to store up knowledge makes living burdensome. This is why highly intellectual people are often impractical <note>original: unpractical</note> and unsuccessful; they have accumulated more knowledge than they have the wisdom and power to apply. Like the miser who starves while surrounded by his gold, these people perish for lack of real understanding.

<summary>This page discusses the relationship between the physical brain and the various levels of mind—conscious, subconscious, and the Christ-mind—explaining that they are all expressions of a single universal Mind. It warns that accumulating intellectual knowledge is useless unless one has the wisdom to apply it.</summary>

### Lesson Nine

Because people think of their stored-up knowledge as a private possession, it has become disconnected <note>original: insulated</note> from the original source <note>original: fount</note> of wisdom and life. As a result, this knowledge has become stale and lacks power.

Within a person is a quality that, when opened, will place them in direct contact with Universal Knowledge, and they can instantly and continuously draw from it for anything they may wish to know. God is our source of wisdom just as he is our source of all we need. The understanding of the Christ-Mind reveals that a person, on their own, knows nothing. Jesus, who developed this Higher Consciousness, claimed that all his knowledge and power came directly from the Father:

> "I cannot do anything on my own; the Father who lives in me performs the works." <note>original: "I of mine own self can do nothing; the Father within me, he doeth the works." (John 14:10)</note>

All that a person really needs is to awaken <note>original: quicken</note> and develop the thinking centers in their consciousness; then the Divine Mind will think through them. This Supreme Mind holds each person at its center as a perfect instrument through which to express its possibilities. The writer of the first chapter of Genesis says that humanity is the "image and likeness" of God. A person is the I-AM-AGE, or the identical "I AM" of the God-Mind in expression. God looks into the mirror of the universe and sees himself as humanity; he gives himself to us, and a person at their highest is God made manifest.

> "Anyone who has seen me has seen the Father." <note>original: "He who hath seen me hath seen the Father." (John 14:9)</note>

Thus, God gives to his image the power to express all that he is. This includes not only a person's ability to think, but also the power to shape and form thought. This formative power of thought requires a distinctive...

<summary>This page explains that human wisdom is not a personal possession but a direct connection to Universal Knowledge. It identifies humanity as the "I-AM-AGE" (image) of God, possessing the divine ability to manifest God's possibilities through the power of thought.</summary>

<keywords>Universal Knowledge, Christ-Mind, Higher Consciousness, Divine Mind, Genesis, I-AM-AGE, God-Mind, formative power of thought, spiritual anatomy</keywords>

First, there is the gathering of the materials, then the mixing, and finally the cutting of the dough, which gives shape to the substance. In thinking, a person accumulates a mass of ideas about substance and life, and through their imagination, shapes them into forms.

Whatever we reflect in our minds becomes a living, active thing, and through it, we are connected with the world around us. Through this imaging faculty, every thought creates a form, and a multitude of thoughts create a multitude of forms. These crowd together around the central I-AM-AGE <note>The author uses "I-AM-AGE" as a wordplay on "image," suggesting that the "I AM" (the divine identity) expresses itself as a form or an "age" in the physical world.</note> and appear as what we call the body. Physiology states that all the organs of the body are made up of cells, and that every cell carries the form and character of its particular organ. The liver is made of a multitude of little "livers," the heart of little "hearts," and so on. The starting point is always an idea, and through the mechanism of the mind (which is often mistakenly called the mechanism of the body), a person forms their own organism. With this key, one can unlock the door of their temple <note>A common metaphysical reference to the physical body as a "temple of the living God."</note> and, in their mind, visit all its various rooms to set the furniture in order.

The imagination has its center of action in the front of the brain and uses what phrenology <note>Phrenology was a popular 19th-century theory that claimed a person's character and mental faculties could be determined by the shape of their skull.</note> calls the perceptive faculties. Imagination is actually the author of these faculties; concepts like size, weight, form, and color are its children. When imagination flashes its light into the cells that make up these organs, they immediately respond to the thought and create forms out of...

This is a reversal of the Creative Law, which dictates that all creations must have their patterns within the mind first. When a person allows their imagination to run wild in an undisciplined way, they bring about such discord in their mind and body that the "flood" of mistaken thoughts <note>original: "error thought"</note> submerges their understanding, and they are drowned in it.

Knowing this fact puts a person on their guard; they direct their imagination so it does not create things in the mind based on impressions forced upon them from the outside. This does not mean that the external world is entirely wrong, or that everything that appears is merely a creation of the limited human mind <note>original: "mortal mind"</note>. Birthmarks have long been recognized as the effect of the mother’s mind <note>Historical context: It was once a common medical and folk belief, known as "maternal impression," that a mother’s intense emotions or experiences during pregnancy could physically mark or shape the unborn child.</note>, and this mental sympathy <note>original: "mental sympathy"; here referring to a psychological or psychic connection.</note> does not end with birth. Here are some actual occurrences: A lady watching her little daughter pass through a heavy iron gate saw it swing shut <note>original: "swing to"</note>. She imagined that it caught and crushed the little one’s fingers, but the child withdrew her fingers before the gate reached them. The mother felt the pain in her own hand, and the next day she found a dark streak across her fingers where she had imagined the child’s were crushed. In a certain secret society initiation, the candidate was told that the word “coward” was to be branded upon his back with a red-hot iron. A piece of ice was used instead, but the promised brand appeared in blistered letters.

So we could cite cases without number to prove the power of the imagination in forming and transforming the body. Also, one mind can suggest a condition to another and produce any desired result, if there is mental receptivity <note>The text ends mid-word: "recep-".</note>...

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the power of the imagination, specifically how mental images and suggestions can manifest as physical health or illness, and how they can be used to transform character.</meta>

<header>IMAGINATION 103</header>

Experiments prove that we are constantly suggesting all sorts of things to one another and achieving results according to the intensity of our imagination. Thus, disease is reflected into susceptible minds by people merely talking about illness as if it were a terrifying reality.

A person can imagine that they have some harmful condition in their body or their life, and through this law of mind, build it up until it becomes manifest. On the other hand, one can use the same power to make good appear on every side. The marks of old age can be erased from the body by mentally seeing it as youthful. If you want to be healthy, do not imagine such useless <note>original: "vain"</note> things as weakness and physical decline <note>original: "decrepitude"</note>. Make your body perfect by seeing perfection in it. The temporary patching up with lotions and external applications is foolish—the work must be a mental transformation. "Be transformed by the renewing of your mind" <note>A reference to Romans 12:2.</note>.

<margin>✓</margin>
The highest and best work of the imagination is in transforming one's character. Imagine that you are one with the Principle of Good and you will become good. To imagine oneself as perfect fixes the idea of perfection in the invisible mind-substance <note>original: "mind-substance"; in metaphysical philosophy, this refers to a spiritual "clay" or energy that responds to and is shaped by human thought.</note>, and all its forces immediately go to work to bring forth that perfection. Paul saw this wonderful law at work in the formation of character through imitating Christ: "But we all, with unveiled faces beholding as in a mirror the glory of the Lord, are transformed into the same image from glory to glory, even as from the Lord the Spirit" <note>A reference to 2 Corinthians 3:18.</note>.

## PERFECTION IN FORM ESTABLISHED

->(To be used in connection with Lesson Nine.)<-

I always see my face <note>original: "countenance"</note> in its divine perfection.

You will keep him in perfect peace whose imagination is focused <note>original: "stayed"</note> on You. <note>A modernization of Isaiah 26:3.</note>

I see perfection in all forms and shapes.

His Son is the radiance <note>original: "brightness"</note> of His glory and the exact representation <note>original: "express image"</note> of His person. <note>A reference to Hebrews 1:3.</note>

I always see the light of the Christ-consciousness <note>A term in metaphysical Christianity referring to the highest state of spiritual awareness and alignment with the divine.</note>.

I am formed anew every day in my mind and body.

## LESSON TEN

### WILL AND UNDERSTANDING

> <image-desc>Ornamental drop-cap letter 'I' decorated with floral patterns and scrolls within a square border.</image-desc>“If anyone chooses to do His will, he will know whether the teaching is from God <note>original: "he shall know of the doctrine"</note>.”—John 7:17.

Humanity expresses <note>original: "manifests"</note> that which exists eternally in Being <note>In this context, "Being" refers to the fundamental, spiritual reality or the nature of God.</note>. We talk about the faculties <note>term: "faculties" refers to the inherent powers or abilities of the mind, such as will, imagination, and understanding.</note> of the human mind as if they belonged to the individual and originated within them. However, humanity exists within the One Invisible Mind. A person may assume they have a mind of their own, but their origin and destiny are rooted in that Original Mind.

Primary <note>original: "Primal"</note> causes are complete, finished, and absolute. Everything that humanity expresses has its origin in a Cause which we name Divine Mind, Spirit, or God. Since this is true in both logic and intuition, it is not difficult to conclude that the manifestation proves the character of the cause. In dealing with human faculties, this relationship between them and the One Mind should not be lost sight of. There is only One Mind, and that Mind cannot be separated or divided because, like the principles of mathematics, it is indivisible. All we can say of this Mind is that it is Absolute, and that all its expressions are, in their essence, just like it. This brings us to a true assessment of humanity; when we speak of the spiritual person, the Christ-Man, or the Son of God, we are referring to this original expression of the Divine Mind.

# LESSON TEN

In analyzing these powers of the mind <term>original: "faculties"</term>, and establishing their relationship within individual consciousness, we should clearly understand that they are never separated from their Source, the Divine Mind. In the biblical text quoted earlier, Jesus refers to two of the powers of humanity and highlights a specific aspect of their relationship. "Will" and "know" designate what we call the Will and the Understanding—fundamental powers of the mind. These powers still exist in the Mind of Being <note>The "Mind of Being" refers to the fundamental, universal reality of God's consciousness.</note>, even though they seem to have been taken on by the individual and appear to grow and expand within their own consciousness as if the person were their source.

Self-consciousness is like an eddy in the ocean: all the elements found in the ocean are also found in the eddy, and every eddy may, in time, receive and give out everything that is in the ocean. As the expression of God’s will, humanity represents the "I AM" identity <note>In this context, "I AM" identity refers to the core spiritual self that is connected to the divine name revealed to Moses, signifying pure existence and self-determination.</note>. This is self-consciousness: the freedom to act without any kind of dictation; a sense of selfhood without being controlled by external causes; the power to create or destroy without limitation; and the ability to build or tear down using a universe of workable possibilities. 

The will is the person. Without absolute freedom of will, a person would be an automaton <note>An automaton is a machine that operates on its own, like a robot, without personal choice or consciousness.</note>. If their will were restricted even slightly in any way, they would not be perfectly free. But we know that God is the Great Unlimited, and humanity—as God's "image and likeness"—must have the same character. Consequently, a person is just as free to act to fulfill their desires as God is. God cannot interfere with human acts, although He may instruct and

The idea that God forces humanity to do certain things cannot be true in even a single instance, because if it were, then humans would not be free agents <term>free agent: an individual with the power to act according to their own choices rather than by necessity or fate</term>. If God interfered with the human will in some matters, it would follow that He could interfere in any and all matters. Both logic and observation clearly reveal that humanity possesses freedom in everything.

Creative thought uses the will to build up individual consciousness. The Lord God, or Jehovah <note>original: *Yahweh*</note> of Genesis, means in the original Hebrew, “I will be what I will to be” <note>This refers to the divine name revealed to Moses in Exodus 3:14, *Ehyeh asher ehyeh*, often translated as "I AM THAT I AM."</note>. Both Jehovah and Jesus mean, in the mind, "I AM." The "I AM" is a person's self-identity. This "I AM" is the center around which a person's entire system revolves. When the "I AM" is established in a clear understanding of its Principle <note>In this context, "Principle" refers to the fundamental spiritual law or the unchanging nature of God.</note>, it is guided in its actions, and those actions are in harmony with Divine Law. This is the union of the Will and the Understanding. In the Scriptures, these are represented by Ephraim and Manasseh, the sons of Joseph. Their inheritance in the Promised Land was joined together, indicating that these mental powers <term>original: "faculties"</term> work in the body from a single brain center. This center is located in the forehead.

The will should never be hindered in its development; instead, it should be strengthened in every way. The idea of “breaking the will” of children is completely mistaken. What is actually needed is the rounding out of their understanding, which—when joined to the will—creates the perfect person. The idea of "giving up the will" should not be thought of as weakening it or making it less powerful. Rather, it means the will should receive instruction and become obedient to that guidance. To strengthen the will and, at the same time, discipline it correctly requires nothing less than divine understanding. Nearly every mistake results from the will acting without the cooperation of its partner, understanding. When the will develops the habit of acting on its own, a person becomes emotional and headstrong. This state of consciousness leads to all kinds of physical ailments <note>original: "bodily discords"</note>. The human family has learned through "hard knocks" that experience is a harsh schoolmaster. To heal effectively, one must instruct the patient and reveal both the cause of the ailment and its cure through the lens of spiritual understanding. All other methods provide only temporary results. Although they may find brief relief through simple mental suggestion or hypnotism, they will never achieve permanent healing until they understand the Divine Law <note>Divine Law: the spiritual principles that govern the universe and human experience</note> governing possessions and align their life with it.

There are people who claim they are being spiritually developed through mediumship <note>mediumship: the practice of purportedly mediating communication between spirits of the dead and the living</note>. This is a mistake. If you believe you are controlled by the will of another and surrender yourself to it, your own will is gradually weakened; if this continues, you will lose control of yourself entirely. The will must be strengthened by being constantly exercised in Divine Understanding. Mesmerism <note>Mesmerism: an early form of hypnotism based on the theories of Franz Mesmer regarding "animal magnetism"</note> weakens the will, whereas spiritual understanding quickens <note>quickens: brings to life or restores vitality</note> it and makes it alive. God never puts anyone to sleep.

> "Awaken, you who sleep, and Christ shall give you light."

I am one with Infinite Understanding." The accumulation of ignorance caused by associating with ignorant minds can be dissolved by the Word <note>the Word: a focused spiritual statement or affirmation used to manifest a change in consciousness</note>. You may truly *know* things simply by maintaining the conviction that you know. This is not egotism, but spiritual knowing. It was from this state of consciousness that Jesus said: "I thank you that you have heard me, and I *know* that you hear me *always*."

<margin>✓</margin>
A very practical application of the Truth regarding the human will can be found in the practice of self-control. Those who attempt to gain control through the personal will alone will fail to reach their goal. We should be free to express the fullness of who we are. Our feelings, appetites, and passions must be disciplined. They should not merely be suppressed or held in check by the will; instead, they are to be elevated and developed through the Christ-Mind <note>Christ-Mind: the divine or enlightened consciousness within a person that is in perfect union with God</note>.

The challenge of self-control is never fully resolved until every part of a person's being comes into alignment with the Divine Will and Understanding. We must understand all of our inner forces before we can establish them in harmony. ### Lesson Ten

"...people are controlled by Law. There is an eternal Law of Justice. I am one with that Law, and I rest in it.”

Among the disciples of Jesus, Matthew represents the Will, and Thomas represents the Understanding. Matthew was the tax collector <note>original: "tax-gatherer"</note> who sat at the gate, representing the executive branch of the government. In the same way, the Will is the executive faculty of the mind and carries out the decrees <note>original: "edicts"</note> of the I AM <note>I AM: the essential spiritual identity or the spark of God within an individual</note>. All thoughts that enter or leave a person's consciousness pass the gate at which the Will sits. If that Will understands its duty <note>original: "office"</note>, it investigates the character and value of every thought and demands a certain contribution <note>original: "tribute"</note> for the benefit of the whole person.

Thomas, representing the Understanding, is described as being under discipline—meaning he is not yet fully enlightened by the Spirit. When the Understanding takes its first steps in Truth, it requires its lessons and accompanying demonstrations to be expressed in terms similar to those of the external world <note>original: "the world without"</note>. When the Christ showed himself to Thomas, Thomas said he would not believe unless he could see the marks of the nails and feel the wound in his side. When this was granted and the visual proof <note>original: "ocular proof"</note> was given to him, and Jesus had said, “Do not be faithless, but believe <note>original: "Be not faithless, but believing"</note>,” Thomas was awakened spiritually and answered, “My Lord and my God.”

Those people who are being educated in Truth through the written and spoken Word will finally arrive at the place where the true light from the Spirit will dawn upon them. Like Thomas, they will eventually see with spiritual understanding and have proof of the reality of the Christ-Mind <note>Christ-Mind: the divine consciousness or the state of being in alignment with God's thoughts</note>.

## JUDGMENT AND JUSTICE

Do not judge, so that you will not be judged. For with the judgment you judge, you will be judged: and with the measure you use <note>original: "measure ye mete"</note>, it will be measured back to you again.—Matthew 7:1-2.

And you shall put the Urim and the Thummim <note>Urim and Thummim: Hebrew terms meaning "Lights" and "Perfections," referring to sacred objects used by ancient Israelite priests to determine God's will</note> in the breastplate of judgment; and they shall be over Aaron’s heart when he goes in before the Lord: and Aaron shall bear the judgment of the children of Israel upon his heart before the Lord continually.—Exodus 28:30.

“Urim and Thummim (Lights and Perfections). These were the sacred symbols (worn upon the breastplate of the High Priest, over his heart) by which God gave divine answers <note>original: "oracular responses"</note> for the guidance of his people in worldly <note>original: "temporal"</note> matters. What they were is unknown; they are introduced in Exodus without explanation, as if they were familiar to the Israelites of that day. Modern Egyptology supplies us with a clue; it tells us that Egyptian high priests in every town, who were also its magistrates, wore around their necks a jeweled gem bearing on one side the image of Truth, and on the other sometimes that of Justice, sometimes that of Light. When the accused was acquitted, the judge held out the image for him to kiss. In the final judgment, Osiris <note>Osiris: The ancient Egyptian god of the afterlife and judge of the dead</note> wears the jeweled Justice and Truth around his neck. The Septuagint <note>original: "LXX." (the earliest Greek translation of the Hebrew Bible)</note> translates Urim and Thummim as ‘Light and Truth.’ Some scholars suppose that they were the twelve stones of the breastplate; others believe they were two additional stones concealed in its folds. Josephus <note>Josephus: A famous first-century Jewish historian</note> adds to these the two sardonyx <note>sardonyx: A variety of onyx stone with reddish-brown and white layers, highly valued in antiquity</note> buttons, worn on the shoulders, which he

<summary>This page introduces the spiritual principles of judgment and justice through biblical mandates and a historical examination of the Urim and Thummim. It compares these Israelite sacred objects to similar symbols of truth and justice used by high priests in ancient Egypt.</summary>

<keywords>judgment, justice, Urim and Thummim, breastplate of judgment, High Priest, Egyptology, Osiris, Septuagint, Josephus, sardonyx</keywords>

--- Page 121 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Urim and Thummim" from the previous page, shifting from historical definitions to a metaphysical interpretation of the Law of Moses and the spiritual faculties of the mind.</meta>

...says it emitted luminous rays when the response was favorable; but the precise way in which the oracles were given is lost in obscurity." — *Bible Glossary of Antiquities*.

<image-desc>Ornate decorative drop cap 'T' with floral and scrollwork designs.</image-desc>The Law as given by Moses is for the guidance of humanity in the evolution of its faculties. The figures, personalities, and symbols represent potentials—both developed and undeveloped—on various planes of consciousness. The High Priest represents the spiritual person officiating between God and mortal humanity. Just as a breastplate in armor protects the most vital parts, with the heart being the center, so the breastplate of the High Priest represented judgment, worn over the heart. The heart signifies love—the emotional <note>original: "affectional"</note> consciousness in a person—and it may be subject to the force of weak sympathy unless it is balanced by another power that provides discrimination, or judgment.

This breastplate featured twelve precious stones representing the twelve tribes of Israel <note>original: "Irsael"</note>, which clearly signifies that the twelve faculties of the mind must be concentrated at this great nerve center, the Solar Plexus <note>Solar Plexus: A complex system of radiating nerves at the pit of the stomach, often considered in metaphysics to be a secondary brain or center of feeling</note>, and all their intelligence must be brought into play in the mind's final judgments. The Urim and Thummim <note>original: "Thummin"</note>—meaning "Lights and Perfections"—which under Egyptian symbolism represent "Truth and Justice," are the prophetic decrees <note>original: "oracular edicts"</note> of the Divine Mind. These are intuitively expressed as a logical result of the Divine Principle: Truth and Justice.

The great judgment day mentioned in the Bible <note>original: "Scripture"</note> refers to a time of separation between what is true and what is false. There is no evidence to support the belief that God sends humanity to everlasting punishment. Modern interpreters of the Bible say that the “hell-fire” referred to by Jesus means simply a state in which purification is taking place.

The word “hell” is not translated with enough clarity to represent the various meanings of the word in its original languages. There are three words from which the English word "hell" is derived: *sheol* (meaning “the unseen state”), *hades* (meaning “the unseen world”), and *gehenna* (meaning “the Valley of Hinnom” <note>Gehenna: A valley outside Jerusalem where fires burned constantly to consume waste; it was used by Jesus as a metaphor for spiritual purification.</note>). These are used in various contexts, and nearly all of them are allegorical. 

In a sermon, Archdeacon Farrar <note>Frederick William Farrar (1831–1903): A prominent Anglican cleric whose scholarly work challenged traditional Victorian views on eternal damnation.</note> said: “There would be the proper teaching about hell if we calmly and deliberately erased from our English Bibles the three words, ‘damnation,’ ‘hell,’ and ‘everlasting.’ Yet I say unhesitatingly—claiming the fullest right to speak with the authority of knowledge—that not one of those words ought to stand any longer in our English Bible, for in our present understanding of them, they are simply mistranslations.” 

This confirms the metaphysical interpretation <note>Metaphysical interpretation: An approach to the Bible that looks for psychological and spiritual truths hidden behind literal or historical descriptions.</note> of the Bible and supports the truth that hell is a figure of speech representing a corrective state of mind. When an error has reached its limit, the law of cause and effect <note>original: "retro-active law"</note> asserts itself. Judgment, being part of that law, brings a penalty upon the person who has done wrong. This penalty is not punishment, but discipline,

## LOVE

> See what great love the Father has given <note>original: "bestowed"</note> to us, that we should be called the children of God; and that is what we are. —I John 3:1.

LOVE, in the Divine Mind, is the idea of Universal Unity. In its expression, love is the power that joins and binds the universe and everything in it together in Divine Harmony.

Among the various faculties of the mind, Love is the central point <note>original: "pivotal"</note>. Its center of mental activity <term>mentation: the process of using the mind; mental function</term> in the body is the **Solar Plexus**. The physical representation of love is the heart, the function <note>original: "office"</note> of which is to balance the circulation of blood in the body. Just as the heart balances the flow of life through the body, love harmonizes the thoughts of the mind.

His book, *The Greatest Thing in the World*, analyzes love and portrays its various activities based on Paul's poem. We quote him as follows:

> "**THE SPECTRUM OF LOVE.** 'Love is a complex thing,' Paul tells us. It is like light. Just as you have seen a scientist take a beam of light and pass it through a crystal prism, and you have seen it emerge on the other side of the prism broken up into its individual colors—red, blue, yellow, orange, and all the colors of the rainbow—so Paul passes this thing, Love, through the magnificent prism of his inspired intellect. It comes out on the other side broken down into its basic elements. In these few words, we have what one might call the Spectrum of Love—the analysis of Love. Will you observe what its elements are? Notice that they have common names; they are virtues we hear about every day; they are things that can be practiced by everyone in every area of life; and how..."

Professor Drummond, in his address upon this chapter to Mr. Moody's students gathered at Northfield, Massachusetts, said: "How many of you will join me in reading this chapter once a week for the next three months? A man did that once and it changed his whole life. Will you do it? *Will you?*"

Love is more than mere affection, and all our words professing our love are of no value unless we have this inner current, which is a real substance. Though we may have the eloquence of men and angels, if we do not have this deeper feeling, it profits us nothing. We should then deny mere conventional, surface affection and set our minds on the very substance of love.

You may be a martyr to the cause of Truth and consume your vitality in good works, yet still be far from love. Love is a force that runs through the mind and body like molten gold in a furnace. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;"The love of money is the root of all evil." <note>1 Timothy 6:10</note> This does not say that money itself is the root of all evil, but rather the love of it. Money is a convenience that saves people many burdens when exchanging items of value. Primitive civilizations used the cumbersome custom of trading products directly without a representative currency, whereas modern progress uses money increasingly as a medium of exchange. Money is, therefore, beneficial to a person of common sense; however, when someone allows themselves to become enamored with it and hoards it, they turn it into their god. The removal <note>original: "erasement"</note> of this idea from human consciousness is part of the metaphysician’s <term>metaphysician</term> work. <note>In this context, a "metaphysician" refers to a practitioner of New Thought or spiritual science who believes that mental and spiritual laws govern physical reality.</note> By trusting in God, we have faith in Him as our Resource, and He becomes a perpetual spiritual supply and support; but by having faith in the power of material riches, our trust is pulled away from God and established in this temporary substance prone to rust and corruption. This point is not clearly understood by those who are hypnotized by the idea of money. When the metaphysician affirms that God is their abundant <note>original: "opulent"</note> supply and support and declares that they have money in abundance, the assumption by others is that they love money and depend upon it in the same way that the devotees of mammon <term>mammon</term> do. <note>original: "mammon," a biblical term for wealth or greed personified as a false god.</note> The difference is that one trusts in the law of God while the other trusts in the ways of mammon. The person who blindly gives themselves up to the pursuit of money acquires a love for it and finally becomes its slave. The wise metaphysician deals with the idea of money and masters it.

This is "Adam." But these three are one because the manifestation rests upon and is sustained by the Idea, and the Idea is encompassed by the Mind that conceives it. Therefore, the reality of Adam is the Lord God, and the omnipresent source <note>original: "Fount"</note> of the Lord God is the One God.

Man is not Adam: Adam is only a part of a person's consciousness. Adam is your intellect, but you transcend the intellect. You form your intellect from the "dust of the ground"—that is, the omnipresent substance—and through it, as a kind of reflecting lens, you give character to your surroundings.

Those familiar with the study of the operations of the intellect tell us that it is constantly making images of the ideas that float into its surroundings. Such a person becomes familiar with certain underlying principles and recognizes them behind their different masks in the constant shifts <note>original: "whirligig"</note> of time. These cycles repeat themselves over and over again, but each time on a slightly higher level. # How Microbes are Made

However, principles do not change—people create their own heaven or hell today just as they did two thousand or two million years ago. In the days of Moses, the Egyptians refused to grant freedom to the Israelites (who represent spiritual ideas), and as a result, they encountered plagues of frogs, lice, locusts, and blood in the earth, air, and water. Today, those who argue for the "Egyptian darkness" of the pure intellect see the same earth, air, and water filled with disease germs, deadly microbes, and destructive microorganisms <note>original: "animalculæ," a term used in early biology for microscopic organisms</note>.

It is now almost universally accepted by physicians that the majority of diseases are caused by tiny forms of life commonly called "microbes" or disease germs. Diseases such as cancer, tuberculosis <note>original: "consumption"</note>, diphtheria, and croup each have a specific microbe that acts as its invisible but active cause. These microbes can be seen through powerful microscopes. Their forms and characteristics have been fully described by experts like Louis Pasteur and Robert Koch, whose treatments <note>original: "antidotes"</note> for these destructive little organisms have been widely publicized. Their remedy focuses on destroying the microbe itself, without attempting to explain its origin. They find these tiny workers busy within the human body and seek to disable them, without asking where they originated or where they might go.

However, a reflective mind is not satisfied with such a superficial approach to these destructive agents. It asks for their cause, but no answer is granted by those who study microbes. Only students of the mind can answer the question regarding the origin of mi— <note>The text cuts off here, likely intended to be "microbes"</note>

Thinking is formative; every thought clothes itself in a life form according to the character given to it by the thinker. # THE I AM IN ITS KINGDOM

The people of the world have always looked upon this as a figure of speech. Even metaphysicians, who have delved into the abstract concepts of the mind, have had vague ideas about heaven being a place within them. They have said it was a state or a condition.

And so it is, but it is also a place. It is not outside of your body today and inside of it tomorrow; nor can it be anywhere else but right at the center of what you call the physical man.

This insistence on the specific location of heaven is a startling proposal to those who have assumed that mind is universal, existing without bounds or limitations.

We are seeking to enter the kingdom of heaven where "all things shall be added unto us," and it is proper that we should know where that heaven is. Everything we currently know about ourselves comes through comparison with "the things that appear" <note>This is a common metaphysical reference to the visible, material world as opposed to the invisible, spiritual reality.</note>. We have a body, which we clearly perceive is moved by an invisible principle called the mind. We have never seen this mind, nor felt it, nor sensed it in any way.

We simply know that certain combinations of thought produce effects upon the physical self <note>original: "sense man"</note>. This all takes place from our center of consciousness, our physical body. Therefore, as far as we are concerned, the mystery of Being is wrapped up in and around what we are accustomed to calling "clay" <note>A traditional metaphor for the human body, referring to the biblical idea of man being formed from the dust of the ground.</note>. Do not misunderstand this proposal and assume that the physical man, as he currently appears to your understanding, is the highest good <note>original: *summum bonum*</note> of existence. This is not the claim. The claim is that it—

## HOW SHALL THE DEAD BE RAISED?

<image-desc>Decorative square woodcut initial 'A' with foliate scrollwork.</image-desc>As disciples of Jesus Christ, we are commanded to "raise the dead." To clearly understand this part of our mission, we should familiarize ourselves with the philosophy of death—what it is and how it came to be.

Death is defined by Webster <note>Refers to Noah Webster’s dictionary, the standard American English reference of the time.</note> as the "permanent cessation of all the vital functions." This, like all definitions derived from observing the physical senses, is quite incomplete. It gives us no idea of the relationship that death has with its polar opposite, life. Nor does it explain the process through which life passes so that it appears to be absent in something that has a "cessation of vital functions." Following this to a final analysis, we find that we must understand life before we can grasp the appearance of its absence in a form called "dead."

In this investigation—as in every other search for Truth that starts from the correct premise <note>The underlying assumption or starting point of an argument.</note>—we find that we can never understand the proper relationship of things by examining the negative side. One could not correct the errors in a mathematical calculation without first understanding the rules governing numbers. Some people think they can learn how to be healthy by studying disease, but they only sink further and further into sickness until they turn around and study health. Then, once they have learned about health, disease no longer holds any interest for them, and they stop studying it.

<summary>This page introduces a metaphysical study of death, arguing that to fulfill the command to "raise the dead," one must first understand the nature of life rather than focusing on the "negative" state of death or disease.</summary>
<keywords>resurrection, definition of death, Webster's Dictionary, vital functions, metaphysics, health vs. disease, spiritual logic</keywords>

In metaphysics, we find that new students insist on having the nature of evil explained to them—how it began and why it has a place in existence, when the source of everything that exists is good. They worry, fret, and beat their brains <note>original: "cudgel their thoughts"; meaning to struggle mentally or think hard about a problem.</note> over this question until, in sheer desperation, they usually give up. So, in our investigation into the nature and role of death, we find that studying it in isolation provides no starting point for even a single fact. It has no foundation in itself. If we have wasted our substance in reckless living <note>original: "riotous living"; an allusion to the Parable of the Prodigal Son, implying the squandering of one's vital energy or moral character</note>, our word goes forth without a vehicle to carry it.

We should be just as careful with the stored-up substance of our consciousness—of which the body is the lowest level—as we are with the thoughts and words we express. If our substance is being wasted on physical cravings, our words will lack life-giving quality. Jesus had overcome "the world, the flesh, and the devil." He had removed the limitations of matter from his consciousness—he rose above the appetites and passions of the animal man <note>original: "animal man"; the base, instinctive, or unevolved side of human nature</note> and dissolved all fear of evil.

Jesus demonstrated the law of God, and his word was filled with power. He became the word of God incarnate <note>original: "incarnate"; the divine word taking on a physical, human form</note>, because he fulfilled all the requirements of the Law.

But this is the privilege of everyone. Whoever dedicates their whole life to the Supreme Good—and through devotion, right thinking, right doing, right acting, pure living, and pure speaking fulfills the law—may have all the power of Jesus. God does not show favoritism <note>original: "is no respecter of persons"; meaning God does not grant special treatment based on status, but treats everyone according to the same universal laws</note>, but is incredibly exact in requiring that we follow the *law* down to the smallest detail <note>original: "the least jot and tittle"; referring to the smallest marks in Hebrew writing, signifying even the tiniest requirements</note>.

So we say that death cannot be explained without first knowing life, and knowing life carries with it a knowledge of God. We find that every time we seek to know the origin of an effect, we must go back to the Supreme Cause before we get a complete explanation.

In the matter of life, we discover by following all the clues given to us in our own experiences that they point to intelligence as well as force. In other words, life falls far short of its mission if it is not balanced by intelligence. Yet thousands who are seeking health—which means seeking more life—have no special desire to become acquainted with God. Many think that health and a full life may be had without him. What people need most of all today is more wisdom—more discretion in how they use the life they already possess. Simply having more life, if it is used with the same old destructive ignorance, would only increase their misery.

Yet God does not dictate what a person’s choice shall be in this or any other action. If someone discovers the law through which life is made manifest <note>original: "manifest"; shown or made plain</note> in their consciousness, they may use it blindly and ignorantly if they so choose. However, they must also endure the result, and this is when people begin their wail of sorrow; they do not like to reap what they have sown.

Death entered our world through the ignorant use of life, and death can only be eliminated by using life wisely. Death is the result of a wrong concept of life and its purpose. In the beginning of humanity's experiments with the powers of Being <note>original: "Being"; the fundamental state of existence or God's nature</note>, they had no concept of death. Their consciousness was whole, and their development <note>original: "unfoldment"; the process of opening up or maturing</note> in wisdom was gradual and orderly. But the desire to experiment took over. Physical sensation was sweet and enticing; it absorbed so much of their attention that they forgot wisdom—they “hid” from their Lord—and the result is an absence from their Eden, which is the Divine Harmony of the Law.

When there is disorder in the moving parts of a machine, it breaks down or flies to pieces. That is exactly what occurred in the human body. When intelligence was no longer fully present in a person's consciousness, there was a lack of harmony, which resulted in such...

<page-num>173</page-num>

<header>HOW SHALL THE DEAD BE RAISED?</header>

A departure from Divine Law <note>original: "Divine Law"; the spiritual principles governing the universe</note> in the use of a faculty <note>original: "faculty"; a natural power or ability of the mind or body</note> actually results in its death. That is, after the violent misuse of a power, there is such a reaction that it enters a comatose state or "sleep of death." Death is the failure of a person to maintain a harmonious life within the body.

In a metaphysical sense, death and sleep are brothers. Life’s action is never completely withdrawn from every part of a form, but there is such a loss of vitality that the outer shell dissolves—a process we call death. However, Jesus declared that death is merely sleep and that the sleeper could be awakened once vitality was restored according to divine order. Jesus said Lazarus was asleep, saying, "I go so that I may awake him out of sleep."

But his disciples did not see deeply; they assumed that Lazarus had simply fallen into a trance or a deep sleep and said, "Lord, if he sleeps, he shall recover" <note>original: "do well"; in this biblical context, it means to be healed or get better</note>.

> "Then Jesus said to them plainly, 'Lazarus is dead.'"

Paul frequently referred to those whose bodies had dissolved as brothers and sisters <note>original: "brethren"</note> who had "fallen asleep."

> The Lord told Daniel, "But go your way until the end; for you shall rest, and stand in your assigned place <note>original: "lot"; one's allotted portion or destiny</note> at the end of the days." —Daniel 12:13.

Our poets, in their inspired moments, have captured this truth, and our literature is full of references to the "sleep of death." In his frequently quoted soliloquy, Hamlet reveals the metaphysics of death to us in a remarkable way:

### To die;—to sleep:—

> To sleep! Perhaps to dream: yes, there is the problem; <note>original: "there’s the rub"; a famous line from Shakespeare's *Hamlet* meaning a difficulty or obstacle</note>
> For in that sleep of death, what dreams may come
> When we have cast off this physical body, <note>original: "shuffled off this mortal coil"; a poetic way of describing dying and leaving the troubles of physical life behind</note>
> Must make us hesitate. <note>original: "give us pause"</note>

We are not afraid to go to sleep at night, yet every time we lie down and fall into a state where we are unconscious of the body, we are acting out the sleep of death in a small way. In one case, the soul leaves the body for a few hours and takes it up again—in the other, the soul leaves the body to decompose; yet it does not fail to return in due time and inhabit a body—so long as it believes in the limitations of the senses. During a single night's sleep, a person with a clear conscience rests peacefully and is strengthened for another day’s experience. But the guilty, anxious, and worried sleeper is haunted by dreams.

The experience of one who sleeps in death is similar. If that person's life has been lived according to the Golden Rule, he "wraps the drapery of his couch about him and lies down to pleasant dreams" <note>This is a reference to the final lines of William Cullen Bryant's poem "Thanatopsis," which encourages the reader to face death with trust rather than fear.</note>. His soul basks in the sunshine of a heavenly world <note>original: "world elysian"; referring to Elysium, the paradise for the virtuous in Greek mythology</note> and his hope of heaven is fulfilled for a time.

This, however, is only the rest that prepares him for another day’s experience in the workshop of Being <note>The "workshop of Being" refers to the continuous process of spiritual evolution and physical life</note>, and like the prophet Daniel, he must again "stand in your assigned place <note>original: "thy lot"</note> at the end of the days" <note>Daniel 12:13</note>. This process is repeated again and again until a person discovers that there is a law of living that prevents <note>original: "obviates"</note> this frequently repeated "sleep of death." That law is revealed to all who seek to do the will of God and thereby fulfill the law of life.

# HOW SHALL THE DEAD BE RAISED?

This is where we find ourselves today. We know that the law of life is based on the activity of the mind <note>original: "mind-action"</note>, and that through the mind we can raise ourselves from a state of spiritual death.

As we explore the mental realm—the source of our thoughts—we find it filled with a vast number of narrow, foolish, ignorant, selfish, and conflicting beliefs. We have grouped these together and named them the "mortal mind" or the "carnal mind" <note>In New Thought and metaphysical traditions, the "mortal" or "carnal" mind refers to the ego-driven, physical perspective that is disconnected from spiritual reality.</note>.

This is where we first begin "raising the dead." Each of these mortal beliefs is a sin. The literal meaning of "sin" is "missing the mark" <note>original: "sin"; the author refers to the Greek word *hamartia*, used in archery to describe missing the target.</note>, and these limitations of the senses fail to hit the target of divine truth. We must shine the light of truth into our consciousness and awaken each of these sleeping thoughts. Some of them may seem impossible to revive at first, and even our most optimistic <note>original: "sanguine"</note> thoughts might lose faith at the prospect and cry out, "Lord, by this time there is a stench" <note>original: "Lord, by this time he stinketh"; a reference to John 11:39 regarding the burial of Lazarus.</note>.

But the power of Christ is with us. "Did I not tell you that if you would believe, you would see the glory of God?" <note>A modernization of John 11:40.</note>

All things are possible for those who believe in the power of God dwelling within humanity, waiting to be revealed through their word. So, speak that word and say to every sleeping belief of the senses, "Lazarus, come forth!" <note>John 11:43; the author uses the resurrection of Lazarus as a metaphor for calling forth spiritual life from a "dead" or limited mindset.</note>

If you do not believe in the power of the Spirit to raise your consciousness from its tomb of earthly superstitions, then of course you might make no effort to do it. But if you have faith that it can be done, *you can do it.*

<summary>This page explains "raising the dead" as a mental process of replacing "mortal" or "carnal" beliefs with spiritual truth. Using the story of Lazarus as a metaphor, the author argues that individuals can resurrect their own consciousness through faith and the power of their words.</summary>

Every kind of mental belief lives within our consciousness and makes a home there. You must guard all the powers of your being if you wish to raise them from the dead. These powers do not stand alone; they depend on one another and must all be brought under the control of the Christ of God.

Raising the dead is the sure and certain work of the true Christian.

# THE DEVELOPMENT OF DIVINE LOVE

> “O Jerusalem, Jerusalem, you who kill the prophets and stone those who are sent to you, how often I would have gathered your children together, just as a hen gathers her chicks under her wings, and you would not!” <note>This quote is from Matthew 23:37 or Luke 13:34, where Jesus laments over the city of Jerusalem.</note>

<image-desc>Ornamental drop cap 'J' containing a bird, possibly a swan, amidst stylized floral and vine motifs.</image-desc> JESUS weeping over Jerusalem is the image of a great love welling up in the heart and flowing out to all the ends of the earth—the love of the Good Father for his erring and willful children.

Such is the love of Christ for his own; such is the love of God through Christ for all creation.

We may talk about the wisdom of God, but the love of God must be felt in the heart. It cannot be described, and one who has not felt it can have no concept of it from the descriptions of others. But the more we talk about love, the stronger it grows in the consciousness; if we persist in thinking loving thoughts and speaking loving words, we are sure to bring into our experience the feeling of that great love that is beyond description—the very love of God.

It is commonly taught and believed that there is but one love—that "God is Love" and that all love comes from him; therefore <note>original: "hence"</note>, all love is God's love.

Love is a divine principle and can be known in its purity by reaching its source <note>original: "touching it at its fountain head"</note>. There, it is not colored <note>original: "tinged"</note> in any way by human formative thought <note>In metaphysical traditions, "formative thought" refers to the idea that human beliefs and mental patterns shape our experience of reality.</note>,

<summary>This page describes the nature of divine love as a tangible experience that can be cultivated through persistent loving thoughts and words, using Jesus’ compassion for Jerusalem as a central illustration.</summary>

Humanity has a faculty through which we receive love from Being—this faculty is commonly called the heart. The heart, however, is only the visible expression of an invisible center of consciousness. The person focused on the senses <note>original: "sense man"</note> sees only the physical heart, but the soul sees an inner faculty within a person through which they may express an attribute of Being. It is the individual who, through their word, calls these powers into activity so that they may manifest God.

Jesus was the orderly man of God, manifesting the attributes of Being under Divine Law. Jesus "called his disciples to him"; that is, through his word, he spiritually awakened <note>original: "quickened"</note> and educated his twelve faculties. Love poured through the heart of a mother who harbors fear in her thoughts has shattered the body of many a delicate child. The thinker must be strong and sure in its grasp of right thoughts.

So we find that these four faculties, when evenly balanced, will form the foundation of a harmonious body and mind.

You must think, and think with faith in both God and yourself—that is **Peter**.

You must think with strength and power—that is **Andrew**.

You must think with judgment and discretion—that is **James**.

You must center all your thought, your strength, and your judgment in Love—that is **John**.

To Peter, the faithful thinker, is given the key to the kingdom of heaven, but he can never open the gate until he has reconciled all the other faculties. To think without strength is to produce weak results—without any real effect.

around him, and said, 'Look, here are my mother and my brothers! For whoever does the will of God is my brother, my sister, and my mother.'” <note>This is a modernization of Mark 3:34-35.</note> This is the love of God in its purity, fresh from the source.

Whenever love is stained by selfishness, we know that <term>error-thought</term> <note>error-thought: Thoughts based on false beliefs or a sense of separation from God.</note> has muddied its clear stream; to that extent, it has stopped representing the purity in which it exists in the Father.

Love is the drawing power of the mind. It is the magnet of the universe, and anyone who thinks in <term>Divine Order</term> <note>Divine Order: The inherent harmony and logical sequence of the spiritual laws of the universe.</note> can gather all the attributes of Being around it.

Many who have discovered the law of true thinking and its effects wonder why <term>supply</term> <note>supply: The manifestation of material needs, such as money or resources, through spiritual practice.</note> does not reach them even after months and years of holding thoughts of abundance. It is because they have not developed love. # THE MINISTRY OF THE WORD

<image-desc>An ornamental drop cap 'T' decorated with intricate floral patterns and scrolling vines within a square border.</image-desc>This question of the Word of God—its character, its role <note>original: "office"</note>, and its relationship to humanity—is one widely discussed throughout the theological world. 

The statement made by John at the beginning of his Gospel has deep metaphysical significance <note>original: "import"</note>, and it has always been a point of confusion <note>original: "stumbling-block"</note> for those who believe in a personal God. Only someone who understands the mind—its laws and its inherent nature <note>original: "inherencies"</note>—can grasp the true relationship between God and His Word as John presents it here. 

This "Word" is often interpreted to mean Jesus Christ of Nazareth. While this is true in a broad application—representing a universal consciousness expressing itself through an individual—it is a limited view of the subject. It does not address the essential points regarding the Word and its relationship to humanity and all of creation. 

John says that "all things were made through him; and without him was not anything made that was made" <note>John 1:3</note>. However, this does not cover the entire point; it fails to state that a vital connection still exists between the things created and their Creator. 

This is where traditional theology has wandered away from the reality of a very present, feeling <note>original: "sentient"</note>, and vitally active Spirit that flows through all things, including humanity. 

It is also here that the very essence of the pure metaphysical doctrine proposed <note>original: "propounded"</note> and demonstrated by Jesus Christ shows its greatest value <note>original: "virtue"</note>.

Herein lies the appropriateness of our claim to him as a forerunner of the doctrine we support. He is our elder brother, and we are indebted to him for the clearest presentation of **Spiritual Science** that has ever been given to the world.

The way a doctrine is presented is of vital importance. Some people think it is only necessary to talk about religion in flowing words and heavenly tones. This is one way, but Jesus did not adopt it. His presentation was unique <note>original: "peculiar"</note> in that it carries with it—and illustrates through its works—a basis more enduring than mere metaphysical theory <note>original: "presumption"</note>.

# THE MINISTRY OF THE WORD

To produce works, there must be a working power. This is exactly what the Word is—the working power of God.

Every process we know has regular, advancing steps from beginning to end, and these steps follow recognized principles.

A student of languages must have intelligence as a base of operation; next, he must have the ability to form mental concepts <note>original: "ideality"</note>; and finally, he must have expression. To leave out any of these factors is to defeat the intended goal.

Who can learn a language without the mental concept upon which to form his ideas? And who can express that language without the word through which to convey that inner concept to a listener?

This is how the Word of God is modeled. It is that which conveys the concepts of the Most High to the world. It is not the Most High in His entirety, but it carries with it the "power behind the throne," because "these three are one"—the Father (Principle), the Son (the Concept <note>original: "Ideal"</note>), and the Holy Spirit <note>original: "Holy Ghost"</note> (the Formative Word).

These three are also mirrored in miniature <note>original: "minimized"</note> within each individual. Through every person <note>original: "ego"</note>, all the powers of the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit are being poured, limited only by the extent that the person recognizes, acknowledges, and takes them as their own. They exist in the world as an ever-present Principle <note>original: "Omnipresent Principle"</note> and have a home everywhere, because they are as common <note>original: "ubiquitous"</note> as the air. No one lives for a moment with—

In the Scriptures, light always means *intelligence*. Therefore, that which shines into the consciousness but is not understood by it is the clear revelation, on the level of Spirit, of that higher truth which Spirit alone understands.

To capture this light in their understanding, a person must rise out of the sensory state and into the realm of free ideas. This is where the *Word* does its work; here it is that “all things were made through him, and without him was not anything made that was made” <note>John 1:3</note>.

Those who have studied the Mind from an independent perspective—that is, those who have opened themselves to the flow of original ideas from Spirit—have discovered, in a way that is unexplainable to the physical senses <note>original: "mortal sense"</note>, that there are things which appear to be creations but are not actually creations at all. Instead, they are temporary formations that lose their cohesion and dissolve when their mental support is withdrawn.

These formations are produced by the human mind <note>original: "mentality"</note> working independently of its sphere of wisdom. They are not permanent because they lack the essential quality of permanence: harmony. There can be no creation without a creator; and since there is only one Creator, there can be only one true creation.

God is the source of all things. From God, through orderly steps involving the Perfect Idea (the Son) and the Wise Builder (the Holy Spirit), all creation comes forth.

The Son (humanity) looks to the Father for all instruction, and the Father responds to these requests by sending the Holy Spirit, equipped with the wisdom and power necessary to perform the work.

Humanity stands within the Godhead <term>Godhead</term><note>The essential nature or essence of the Divine, often viewed as the Trinity of Father, Son, and Holy Spirit.</note> as the imaging faculty <term>imaging faculty</term><note>The creative power of the mind to visualize and give mental shape to abstract ideas.</note>. The Spirit knows how reality should be formed to be enduring, and this knowledge is shared with humanity, along with the power to create, when we look for it and acknowledge it. Therefore, when people ignore the wisdom of the **Spirit** and proceed to build their world independently, they create many states and conditions that are not truly "made" at all; they are merely malformations and must necessarily fall to pieces because of their own lack of proportion.

All states of existence are mental states. There is nothing else in all the universe, visible or invisible. Whoever imagines anything else is throwing crude pictures from an uninspired mind onto the screen of their universe. Such pictures last for a short time <note>original: "season"</note>, but their own internal disharmonies <note>original: "discords"</note> lead to their final destruction.

So, in the very nature of things, a way must exist by which humanity may form its consciousness in harmony and resulting permanence. That way is in and through the acknowledgment of the **Holy Spirit**, the **Word of God**.

This is the quality of Being that knows. It is *pure knowing*, and whoever cultivates it becomes so filled with understanding that they intuitively perceive the truth of every question or proposition submitted to them. Such a person does not have to study books or have experience in the realm of physical things.

# YOU MUST BE BORN AGAIN

> "And no one has ascended into heaven, except he who descended out of heaven—the Son of Man." —John 3:13 (Rotherham translation).

<image-desc>Decorative initial letter J inside an ornamental square frame featuring floral patterns and vine-like flourishes.</image-desc>

JESUS said, "Unless a person is born of water and of the Spirit," and, "Whatever is born of the flesh is flesh, and whatever is born of the Spirit is Spirit." Who and what is this being that is subject to so many births?

This important, invisible essence that takes on these Protean <note>original: "Protean" - changing frequently or easily; versatile. Derived from Proteus, a Greek sea god who could change his shape at will.</note> forms is humanity, according to Jesus. But what is a human being?

Plato told his students that a good description of a man was a "biped without feathers." When Diogenes learned of this definition, he got a chicken, and after plucking its feathers, released it before Plato’s class with the words, "Behold Plato’s man!" This is a particularly fitting illustration of the dishonorable <note>original: "ignoble"</note> end of all definitions that limit <note>original: "circumscribe"</note> humanity to physical form alone.

Jesus was clearly referring to an invisible essence that was born of the flesh, then born again of the Spirit; the implication <note>original: "inference"</note> is that this essence was capable of an infinite number of experiences in birth and rebirth.

What is this invisible something that says, "Before Abraham existed, I am" <note>John 8:58</note>? Who are you—the one who was born into this cycle of experiences that you are now passing through—and where did you come from? What is it within you that says "I am"?

# YOU MUST BE BORN AGAIN

When your voice says "I am," does it do so on its own authority, or is it moved by an invisible One? Who is this invisible One, and what is his relation to the voice through which he speaks? These are the most important questions that were ever put to any school on earth. When we begin to consider them, in even the most basic way, we are entering the realm of the gods.

Over the entrance to the Greek temple was written "Know thyself" <note>original: *Gnothi Seauton*—the ancient Greek aphorism inscribed on the Temple of Apollo at Delphi.</note>, and it is always written over every door that opens from ignorance to wisdom. "Know yourself"—know who and what you are, where you came from, what you are doing here, and where you are going.

If you want to know all this, meditate upon the "I AM."

Your mind returns to Moses and Jehovah—you think of a mighty "I AM" far back in history. You do not connect that far-away "I AM" that inspired Moses with your own little everyday "I am" that struggles in the "struggle for survival" <note>original: "brawl for bread"</note>. Yet there is only one I AM. It cannot be divided into parts; it is Principle <term>Principle: In this metaphysical context, Principle refers to the fundamental, unchanging divine law or the source of all existence.</term>. That which says "I am" in all men, women, and children is identical. It is like the mathematical *one*. All the combinations of figures that were ever conceived are only repetitions of this single digit. It is the *son* of the principle of mathematics. It is inspired by its principle, and all the possibilities of that principle are open to it.

Your I AM is the Son of the God Idea, and all the possibilities of the Principle through that idea are open—

This law of sequence is the stabilizing force <note>original: "balance wheel"</note> of the universe. Like all laws that are inherent in Being, it is good.

The ego can have any experience that it wills to have. If it wills to indulge in physical sensation, a state where sensation is celebrated <note>original: "holds high carnival"</note> is provided. If its appetite for sensation is satisfied, other states are open to it; it may be "born of the Spirit."

"...my Father who is in heaven <note>original: "which is in heaven"; in Early Modern English, "which" was commonly used for people as well as objects.</note>, the same is my brother and sister and mother."

The **I AM** <note>The true spiritual self or the spark of divine consciousness within an individual.</note> was born into the flesh through desire, and it is desire that keeps it in the consciousness of the physical body. The five senses are simply avenues of one great central desire—sensation. The I AM desired to experience sensation, and the five senses are the five established avenues through which it enjoys that experience.

Sensation is not an evil, except when you choose to let it crawl on its belly through purely physical channels. It is the "serpent" that deceives man when he turns it outward toward mere appearances <note>original: "seeming"; the author refers to the illusory nature of the external material world.</note>—hearing, seeing, feeling, tasting, and smelling. In this "wilderness of sense," Moses lifted it up. Moses represented the law which the I AM sent forth.

You must create a law for this "serpent" that is holding you in the sensations of the flesh. You desire to be born into the Spirit, but you cannot rise out of the physical state. Something binds you down. Like a captive balloon, you are tugging at the guy ropes that fasten you to the earth.

Mind is the only causative power. It creates and destroys all laws through words. The I AM exists within the mind and formulates the words that set the mind in motion.

If you are bound to the flesh, the cords that hold you are solidified <note>original: "concreted"; meaning hardened or made material.</note> words. If you want to be unbound, it must be accomplished through words. These cords are states of consciousness that you must dissolve. This dissolving process is done through words expressing denial—or negations <note>In this spiritual context, "negations" or "denials" are a practice of mentally rejecting the power of physical limitations or negative beliefs.</note>.

## OBEDIENCE

<image-desc>An ornamental drop-cap letter 'B' at the beginning of the text, featuring a woodcut-style illustration of a seated figure among foliage.</image-desc>

BEFORE the descent of the Holy Spirit upon us, we live in the intellect, and our little world is bounded by the thinking faculty. What our ancestors thought is the pattern after which we shape our own thinking. To anyone who claims a higher source <note>original: "fount"</note> of wisdom, we say, "Are you greater than our father Jacob, who gave us the well and drank from it himself, along with his children and his livestock?" <note>A reference to John 4:12, where the woman at the well questions Jesus’s authority compared to traditional lineage.</note>

Thinking is a process in the mind. All processes come to an end. Every thought has its premise, its stage of action as a reasonable proposition, and its conclusion. So the *I* <note>The author uses "I" to refer to the individual ego or sense of self.</note> that allows the sphere of its existence to be encompassed by the limited thinking faculty follows the process of the <term>syllogism</term> <note>A form of logical reasoning where a conclusion is drawn from two assumed premises (e.g., All men are mortal; Socrates is a man; therefore, Socrates is mortal).</note>, and believes birth, life, and death to be the major premise, minor premise, and conclusion of existence. Instead of recognizing the power to think as simply one faculty of the mind, the *I* assumes it to be the whole of the mind and the entirety of itself. This identification of the free *Self* <note>original: "I"</note> with its own creations brings about a world of illusions. Instead of achieving through the <term>equipoise</term> <note>A state of balance or equilibrium.</note> of effort, it sees no way of reaching a goal except through violent and continued action. To such a person, existence is not a joyous dominion over many obedient powers, but the state of being a rebellious slave to a single one.

To be ushered into life, to blindly toil for a few years...

<summary>This page argues that relying solely on the intellect leads to a limited, logical view of life (birth, life, and death) rather than the spiritual dominion offered by the Holy Spirit.</summary>
<keywords>Obedience, Holy Spirit, Intellect, Syllogism, Jacob’s Well, Spiritual Consciousness, Logic vs Spirit</keywords>
