<page-num>217</page-num>

# OBEDIENCE

To move through life's fitful maze and then vanish into darkness is surely not the method of a wise design. Life must mean more than this, and it does mean more. The individual is the builder, and to him is given all the materials out of which to construct the temple in which he dwells. He builds in wisdom or ignorance, according to his obedience—his receptivity to the sphere of intelligence within him.

Simon, the first disciple of Jesus, represents a receptive attitude of mind. Simon means *hearing*—listening for the inner voice, and obedience *to* it when it says, “Launch out into the deep, and let down your nets for a catch <note>original: "draught"</note>.” When the thinking faculty is obedient and does as it is told, it is always rewarded with a great “*multitude of fishes*,” or new ideas. It is then counted worthy to be a disciple of the Master <note>The author refers to Jesus, or the "Christ-consciousness," as the Master.</note> and its name is changed to Peter. Faith, the substance of thought, then becomes the rock upon which the body temple is built. If you are living in your thinking faculty intellectually—if you believe only in birth, life, and death—you must come out of that belief. You are not exercising your rightful authority <note>original: "dominion"</note>, but are subject to one of your own powers.

You are Spirit, the Son of God, and your place is at the right hand of the Father. To realize this is to call down upon yourself the baptism of the Holy Spirit. After this baptism, you no longer labor as a carpenter or fisherman, but begin to gather together your disciples—the powers of your mind. This gathering together of your powers is an orderly process, and you will find that it pro-

# TALKS ON TRUTH

This process proceeds directly along the lines established by Jesus when he chose his disciples, as recorded in Matthew 4:18 and Mark 1:16. Your first power is the faculty of thinking, represented by Simon; and right alongside him is strength, "his brother Andrew." That is, you discover that your thinking creates your world, and that you also possess the power or strength to control your thoughts and make your world conform to your ideas. Then you separate the *I* <note>The author uses "I" to refer to the "I AM" or the true spiritual identity of the individual, distinct from the wandering mind.</note> from the faculty of thinking, taking control and directing that power according to your wisdom. But true Wisdom comes from the Spirit. "There is a Spirit in man, and the breath <note>original: "inspiration"</note> of the Almighty gives him understanding" <note>Job 32:8</note>.

After you have separated your *I* from the thinking faculty, you are no better off than before unless you recognize that all wisdom comes from the Spirit. You can experience flashes of understanding at any time, but the clear light of the Supreme Being will shine steadily upon you only when you are obedient and receptive to its guidance <note>original: "monitions"</note>. The record states that Jesus prayed often; he sought in every way to do the Father’s will, even to the point of suffering the greatest public shame <note>original: "ignominy"</note> in order to deliver the message he had for humanity. He always listened for the "inner voice," and was obedient to it in his humble and modest work among the lowliest classes of people. To do the will of the Father was his highest aim, because his success depended entirely upon knowing that will. ...recognize that all wisdom and power come from the Spirit—and that wisdom is "given" to man. The highest development of spiritual discernment sees the *I* <note>The author uses "I" to refer to the "I AM" or the true spiritual identity of the individual, distinct from the ego.</note> as possessing nothing of its own, but as the *user* of all things that the Father has.

The relation between God and man is very similar to that existing between a cooperative colony <note>A community where resources are shared for the common good, a popular social and economic model in the late 19th century when this was likely written.</note> and its members. Everything the colony owns is for the use of each member to the full extent of their ability to use it wisely, but they must not attempt to hoard the colony's belongings or claim them as their exclusive property. To know how to establish this relation between Father and Son is the goal of every person, for only through its establishment can their happiness come. Then, the first thing to do—after the *I* has come to an understanding that it is given charge of various powers—is to know how to properly develop them, and finally use them in the grand scheme of creation.

<margin>✓</margin>
Here arises an extremely intricate and interesting point. Can it be rightly said that man possesses any powers? We say that we have judgment, love, and so on; but is it not true that these belong to God, and are merely ours to use in the achievement of an objective in the plan of creation, which has not yet been revealed by the Father? This must be the conclusion of any logical consideration of the matter. Man is given "dominion" <note>original: "dominion"; meaning stewardship or authority over something rather than absolute ownership.</note> over all things, but possession is not transferred. Thus, you do not even possess your own body—it belongs to God. If it is sick or discordant in any way, the issue must be in your *idea* of the body and not in the real body itself. All of God's creations

The Father allows you to use His substance and intelligence to build shadows around what is real, but you eventually learn through experience that these are merely shadows—though you could have known this through a shorter way. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;When you carefully examine your burdens, you will find they come from an idea of possession. For example, you think you have dependents who must be provided with the necessities of life. Your belief in their claim on you comes from your assumption that they have no other protector. Then, through the mental freedom your mind experiences, greater resources flow to you and those you care about from unexpected directions. We do not abandon our friends or stop caring about them; instead, we recognize that they are equal to us in the Supreme Mind. Through that recognition, they are freed from a mental dependence—

...healing <note>This completes the word "healing" from the previous page.</note> of our diseases, but are unwilling <note>original: "loth"</note> to sacrifice the mental habits that cause them. The carnal mind <note>original: "mind of the flesh"</note> knows that its existence depends upon keeping the *Self* <note>original: "I"</note> in its bonds. It begs that the disharmony which its ignorance has produced in the body may be quickly erased without disturbing its own control. Hence, the cry goes up from all over the land, "Heal me! Heal me! Just as Jesus of Nazareth healed those who came to him, but do not ask me to change my ideas."

Moses represents the progressive law of the mind, working out its salvation through obedience to the Spirit. In its "Egyptian darkness" <note>This refers to a state of spiritual ignorance or being enslaved by material desires, symbolized by the biblical captivity in Egypt.</note>, the mind cannot see the way out; it cannot see except through the eye of spiritual perception. Some people mistake spiritual perception for the reality itself and refuse to take the second step of spiritual science <note>original: "science"; here referring to the practical application of divine laws</note>, which is the organic realization of the truths perceived in the mind. This second step is a process of intricate building—stone by stone—of a living temple in which the Spirit resides forever. No one can undertake this construction of a spiritual body until he has committed <note>original: "covenanted"</note> to follow the directions of the Spirit as they are revealed to him from day to day. If he depends upon teachers, healers, books, or the experience of others, he is like a contractor who starts to build according to the design furnished by his architect, but instead of consulting that design and its author at each step, looks here, there, and everywhere for advice on what to do.

The image and likeness of our spiritual body is as thoroughly defined within us as the tree is within the acorn.

Does the acorn consult anything outside of itself regarding how it should produce a tree? Certainly not. It simply rests in the Spirit and unfolds from moment to moment, moved by the impulse within. Exactly the same law is at work in bringing forth the God-man <note>original: "God-man"; a term used to describe the realization of a divine nature within a human being</note>. The external, striving, wandering will must stop its restless seeking on the outside and rest at the center. It must be obedient to that center and learn the language of the Spirit. Moses was a shepherd for forty years before he was competent to lead his people out of servitude. He learned the language of the Father in his hours of solitude, and he knew, without doubting, when he was called to go forth. So we must, every one of us, find the Father consciously in our own inner temples. We must go there day after day and ask for guidance. Mere denials and affirmations <note>These are specific spiritual practices: "denials" involve rejecting the power of negative circumstances, while "affirmations" involve declaring spiritual truths.</note> will not do it. God is Spirit. Spirit is Mind, and Mind *knows*. It is not an abstract concept that exists in a vacuum to be summoned by some magic formula, but it must be cultivated and communed with as a child communes with its parent.

Thus, the reality of living is to live as Jesus of Nazareth lived—at one with the Father. If our ideas of God are only what someone has told us, or what we learned in a course of science lessons <note>This likely refers to "Divine Science" or "Christian Science" lessons, which were popular spiritual curricula in the late 19th century.</note>, we have not found him. He is the one inner plane, the visibility of the soul. We must know him as nearer, dearer, and closer in consciousness than father, mother, wife, husband, or friend. He must be to us the indwelling love and intelligence that leap forth at every word we speak and every thought we think.

Our lives will spring up with new vigor, and the glow of youth will return to our faces, once we realize that the eternal fountain of life bubbles up forever within our own souls.

# THE CHURCH OF CHRIST

> He came to his own, and his own did not receive him.
> —John 1:11.

<image-desc>Ornate decorative initial 'T' featuring a figure and intricate scrollwork.</image-desc>
The pure doctrine of Jesus Christ has never been popular with those who prefer formality and ritual in religion.

<margin>✓</margin>
The disciples of Jesus Christ were from the ranks of the common people, unschooled in the traditional knowledge of the Scribes <note>original: "Scribes"; professional scholars and copyists of Jewish Law who often clashed with Jesus over interpretation.</note> and without any social or religious reputation. They, in turn, became filled with the Holy Spirit and performed extraordinary works of healing and teaching, yet their converts did not largely come from traditional religious circles. It was the “common people” who listened to them and their Master with joy. The aristocracy and the organized church opposed them at every turn. They were stoned, <term>quartered</term> <note>A brutal method of execution where a person's body was divided into four parts.</note>, and burned, and their doctrines never became a popular religion. Pure Christianity was literally suppressed and lost in less than three hundred years after the crucifixion <note>The author is likely referring to the "Constantinian Shift" in the 4th century, when Christianity became the official state religion of the Roman Empire and integrated many pagan customs.</note>. What is called Christianity today is a combination of paganism, ancient Israelite rituals, and the literal words of Jesus’ doctrine without its true spirit.

<margin>✓</margin>
This diverse mixture became acceptable because it was sanctioned by kings and enforced as the official state church. Because it contained a little bit from all existing religions, it offered a sense of comfort to the worshipers who were forced into it from various sects, and thus it quickly became popular.

<margin>✓</margin>
It is not the true doctrine of Jesus Christ, however, and...

# THE CHURCH OF CHRIST

The true church has never existed in any of its many historical forms or sects. Occasionally, a gleam of truth has reached spiritually awakened devotees, leading them to break away from the institution and establish newer, higher standards of truth. However, all of these have fallen far short of the original doctrine set forth by Jesus and his disciples.

Jesus Christ never organized a church on earth, nor did he authorize anyone else to do so. He said to Peter, "On this rock I will build my church." He did not tell Peter that he was to be the head of the church, followed by a line of popes. He said, "I will build *my* church." Jesus Christ remains the head of his church, and its only organization exists in the Spirit. Anyone who attempts to organize it on earth using creeds, <term>tenets</term> <note>The core principles or beliefs of a religion or philosophy.</note>, or textbooks of any kind or description as authority, is acting in direct opposition to his word and example. He provided only one guide and one source from which his followers should receive their inspiration:

> "the Holy Spirit, whom the Father will send in my name; he shall teach you all things and bring to your remembrance everything I have said to you" <note>John 14:26.</note>.

The <term>puerile</term> <note>Childishly silly or trivial; the author finds the argument weak or immature.</note> claim that this promise was intended only for his immediate disciples is hardly worth considering, because there are so many texts in which he plainly states that his ministry and words are for the whole world. In the very same chapter, he said:

> "The one who has my commandments and keeps them is the one who loves me; and the one who loves me will be loved by my Father, and I will love him and reveal <note>original: "manifest"</note> myself to him" <note>John 14:21.</note>.

<summary>This page argues that Jesus Christ never intended to establish an earthly, organized church with human leaders or creeds, but rather a spiritual union guided solely by the Holy Spirit. The author rejects the authority of religious institutions, claiming they contradict Jesus's original teachings.</summary>

This revelation of Truth directly from the Spirit is the rock upon which the one and only church of Jesus Christ is built—all other authorities are <term>spurious</term> <note>original: "spurious"; meaning false, fake, or not proceeding from the true source.</note>.

It is evident from the accepted words of Jesus himself that the one and only true church of Christ has no authority or head on earth. As far as we know, he never authorized the history of his life as recorded in the Gospels; yet, accepting them as such a history, they clearly support the claim of a spiritual church, with only the Holy Spirit as the <term>mediator</term> <note>An intermediary or go-between; the author argues that no priest or institution is needed to reach God.</note> between man and God. It is evident that Jesus saw the tendency among people in the past to turn the Scriptures into idols, and it was his aim to do away with that sort of <term>idolatry</term> <note>The worship of physical objects or written texts instead of the living Spirit.</note>. Instead of a command to “search the scriptures,” Jesus actually scolded the <term>Pharisees</term> <note>A Jewish sect at the time of Jesus known for strict, literal adherence to religious law.</note> in John 5:39 with these words: “You search the scriptures because you think that in them you have eternal life; and yet these are the very things that testify about me.”

It is this constant binding of thoughts to some external authority—whether in a book, creed, or tradition—that keeps people in bondage to the lower world. When the mind is perfectly free to search out the higher truths of existence, a sense of vigor and vitality flows into the consciousness. This sets in motion all those rigid, "crystallized" thoughts, and fresh life stirs the entire person. ...passions conceived by some well-meaning but ignorant "church father" <note>original: "church-father"; a reference to early Christian theologians whose writings established traditional church doctrine.</note> of bygone ages, the open mind flows out in its own natural freedom. The same is true for all questions of doctrine <term>doctrine</term> <note>A set of beliefs or set of principles held and taught by a church.</note> that make up the usual business of inherited religion. What our ancestors debated for a lifetime, fought bitter battles over, and left unresolved, the free-minded person understands in a single moment of consideration. He sees through it with perfect accuracy because his perspective is far removed from the narrow bigotry grafted by creeds and dogmas into the impressionable mind of a young church member.

The human mind is like a clear stream flowing from a high mountain. At its source, nothing exists to corrupt or distort it; but as it flows into the realm of experience, it encounters the obstacles of doubt and fear. It is here that dams are built and its course is diverted in many directions.

Anyone who creates a creed, anyone who writes a book claiming to be an infallible guide for humanity, or anyone who organizes a church that tries to save people from their evil ways through rules and principles is an enemy to mankind. This is also true of anyone who attempts to offer any substitute for the one omnipresent Spirit of God dwelling in each of us.

Yet those very things are the first things attempted by a mindset that is not in constant touch with the flow of the Father. Humans are organizers by nature. It is our function within the Divine <note>original: "God-head"; the essential nature or condition of being God.</note> to give form to the potential—

## Page 232

...qualities <note>original: "alities"; completing the word "qualities" from the previous page.</note> of the Principle. It is through our conscious ego that the Father makes himself manifest to us as Infinite Externality <term>Infinite Externality</term> <note>The concept that God is expressed outwardly through the entire physical universe, as well as inwardly.</note>. The inner and the outer are one only when we recognize that we draw all our life, substance, and intelligence from the Infinite Spirit flowing up within us.

Many people have realized that the true church of Christ is a state of consciousness within us, but few have gone so far in that realization as to know that a temple is also being built in the actual body of every man and woman. In this temple, the Christ holds religious services at all times. "You are the temple of the living God" <note>original: "Ye are the temple of the living God"; a reference to 2 Corinthians 6:16.</note> was not just a symbolic name, but a statement of architectural truth. Through a transformation of the body’s physical substance, the power of thought—under the direction of the Christ—constructs a new body. It breathes an atmosphere and is thrilled with a life energy more real than that of the outer physical form. When someone who has truly entered the church of Christ feels this spiritual body stirring within them, they understand what Paul meant when he said, "There is a natural body, and there is a spiritual body" <note>original: 1 Corinthians 15:44.</note>.

Most of the opposition to the church of Christ comes from those who have never felt this spiritual body moving within them, and they refuse to believe the experiences of those who have. They live only in an intellectual-spiritual state; when the Holy Spirit begins to organize a dwelling place within them, they reject it.

## Page 233

...recognition <note>original: "...i recognition"; likely "intellectual recognition," continuing from the previous page's discussion of those who only understand things on a surface level.</note>, and call it “mortal mind” <term>mortal mind</term> <note>A term used in metaphysical thought to describe the limited, error-prone human intellect that is disconnected from divine truth.</note>, “the devil,” or “an unclean spirit.”

It is this blasphemy against the Holy Spirit <note>original: "Holy Ghost"</note> that Jesus said could not be forgiven. Everything that a person does or has done the Father freely forgives, except for the cursing of his Holy Spirit by calling it an unclean spirit. He who understands the law of mental action can easily see why this cannot be forgiven. ## THE LORD’S BODY

<image-desc>A decorative drop cap 'T' initiates the first paragraph, featuring intricate scrollwork and floral patterns within a square frame.</image-desc>THE teaching of Christianity is that the human race was originally in a beautiful garden, a state of consciousness described as paradise. Here were placed before them two ways of attaining knowledge—one through experience, the other through the inspiration and guidance of God; and they chose the divergent, or hard way. They followed Satan, thinking that through experience they would gain wisdom and pleasure, and in their ignorance they fell short of the law. They did not know how to take advantage of the forces of the mind, and the result was the death of their bodies.

But there was a promise of restoration: that people should come back into that paradise or place where eternal joy and satisfaction exist; and that through Jesus Christ, the original life of humanity should be restored. Jesus Christ understood the Law of God and came to show us the way to live our lives, to resurrect ourselves out of sin and death into immortality. He resurrected his body and promised that those who keep his sayings and follow his law should do likewise; and yet nearly two thousand years have passed and no one has demonstrated the resurrection as far as we know. The teaching has been sidetracked and misunderstood. The popular Christianity of the day tells us that this resurrection is of the soul; that it is to take place after death, or in some future time, and that everyone must die.

# THE LORD'S BODY

Now a new consciousness, a new understanding of this great teaching of Jesus Christ is needed. We are beginning to understand it scientifically. Our physical scientists are showing us in their laboratories that life should be continuous. They tell us that the functions of our bodies are self-perpetuating if rightly directed. There is no reason why they should be destroyed. All around us are the forces that enter into these bodies, and the elements that are found in chemistry are also in the body of flesh.

Why then have we failed for these two thousand years? Because we have not understood and applied the Divine Law. The early disciples evidently caught sight of the great truth that humanity has within itself resurrecting power to overcome disease and sickness, but it gradually fell away and mankind has returned to the old adverse thought.

We must first reduce our religion to *facts*. What do we *know* about it? It is “by their fruits you shall know them.” The fruit of your thought is your body, and you can judge your thought by the character of your body. So also you can change your body by changing your thoughts. Then here is the key to the situation. To resurrect this body we must change our thoughts. Every thought must be in accord with absolute truth, and there must be no adverse thought. We must sepa-
<margin>x</margin>
rate ourselves from all thought of sickness, weakness, and death. They must have no part in the consciousness of the one who would follow Jesus Christ.

# TALKS ON TRUTH

Divine metaphysicians take special care to be logical in their reasoning. They hold that all truth has its origin in the Divine Mind. Whatever we can conceive of as being true must work itself out in creation, and if the creation seems to fall short of divine perfection in any way, it is a fault on our part; either we are not seeing the whole, or we are lacking in understanding. And if we hold to our logic that the Good can create nothing but good, it will bring us to the right conclusion, and the manifestation will always prove itself. Holding to this logic of the mind, and the conclusions of the mind, we find that there are two creations—the Divine Mind idealizes that which it afterward brings forth, just as a man works out an invention in his mind before he makes the model. God is the all-potential Mind. God creates first in thought, and His idea of creation is perfect, and that idea exists as a perfect model upon which all manifestation rests. The body of man must rest upon a Divine Body Idea in the Divine Mind, and it logically follows that the inner life, substance, and intelligence of all flesh are perfect. But you say, "I do not have a perfect body; my body is not the perfect idea, because I can see it is material." It may be that you do not understand; that you do not discern the "Lord's Body," which lack, Paul said, was the cause of weakness, sickness, and death.

We have a perfect body in mind, and that perfect mind-body is expressing itself through our I AM, or the Lord God in us; and it is bringing itself into manifestation just as fast as we will let it; just as fast as we per-

substance so pure that no disintegrating force can be found in it. This gives an importance to the body beyond the usual estimation. People think that the salvation of the soul is the goal of the Christian life, but Jesus and Paul emphasized the ability of man to "lay it down and take it up," even this "Temple of the living God."

Can we save our bodies from death? Yes. By seeing them as the very temples of God; and that means more than looking at them as if you were looking through a telescope. ## NEW THOUGHT

<image-desc>Ornamental drop cap 'N' decorated with stylized leaf and vine patterns within a square woodcut-style frame.</image-desc>NEW THOUGHT is the common name for a complex, and often contradictory, mass of metaphysical doctrines that have emerged in the past few years. No new truth has been presented by any of them; they are simply new ways of thinking about old truths. People who have been religiously educated from the *Book of Common Prayer*—where all the points of doctrine are clearly set forth and nothing is left to the imagination—are panic-stricken when they encounter this New Thought medley. Unless they are well-grounded in Principle, they go right back into the church or gravitate toward some of the New Thought imitations of the orthodox churches. It is only the brave, fearless souls who boldly venture out to meet and seize the truth from this army of free thinkers. Truth includes everything, and the broader the field, the greater the harvest of Truth. What seems to be a contradiction is only the reports of those who see from various levels or points of view, much like people climbing a mountain.

New Thought followers are striving to know the Truth as it is revealed directly from the source. The time is near when the promise shall be fulfilled—

> “And they shall not teach every person their fellow citizen,
> And every person their brother, saying, ‘Know the Lord’:
> For all shall know me,
> From the least to the greatest of them.”

<vocab>New Thought, metaphysics, Book of Common Prayer, Principle, free thinkers, Truth</vocab>

<summary>The author defines New Thought as a modern framework for understanding eternal metaphysical truths through direct revelation rather than traditional church doctrine. He argues that while the variety of these new ideas may seem contradictory, they actually represent different perspectives on a single, all-encompassing Truth.</summary>
<keywords>New Thought, metaphysics, Book of Common Prayer, Principle, free thinkers, direct revelation, spiritual perspective, religious doctrine</keywords>

--- Page 254 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of New Thought, transitioning from the movement's definition to the necessity of individual spiritual authority over traditional religious hierarchies.</meta>

->TALKS ON TRUTH<-

In medieval times, the chiefs and barons assumed they were divinely appointed to govern their fellow men, and their word of authority was accepted by the less assertive, who became their serfs. Similar conditions have existed in an even larger way in religion. Deeply involved in worldly matters, the great majority of people have given no independent thought to religion. What the priesthood has said about the few fragments of spiritual experiences left by the prophets has been accepted as the entirety of God’s plan for religious salvation. But now a universal demand has emerged for a better understanding of spiritual things, and the great activity in the religious world today is the first awakening of this new desire of the soul to know for itself whether the doctrines preached as authoritative are true. To reach this knowledge, it is not safe for a person to trust their information to others; they must know for themselves.

This, then, is really the foundation of the New Thought movement: that each and every one of its followers shall be their own priest and prophet, and stand alone with the Inner Wisdom as their sole guide in religious matters. But not everyone is living up to this liberating doctrine. There are many leaders and many schools dividing their followers into various degrees of limitation. These are the natural divisions of the inner thought planes finding outward expression, and are, to some extent, necessary in the current stage of human development.

There are those in New Thought who are seeking to

<summary>The author explains that New Thought encourages individuals to seek direct spiritual knowledge through their own Inner Wisdom rather than relying on religious authorities. This shift marks a transition from medieval-style dependency on the clergy to a modern era of individual spiritual realization.</summary>
<keywords>New Thought, Inner Wisdom, religious authority, priesthood, spiritual independence, human development, doctrines, individual revelation</keywords>

# NEW THOUGHT

carry their earthly possessions into the kingdom of heaven. They are trying to use the newly discovered powers of the mind to build up humanity on the old foundations. They proclaim the universality of the One Life and Intelligence, and that all things necessarily resting upon God must be good. This logic is so plausible that a very large number of New Thought followers are not using spiritual discrimination or spiritual independence, but are following the path pointed out by some enthusiastic discoverer of half-truths. Thus, there are sharp divisions based upon different understandings of the explanations of the Divine Law in New Thought circles.

What may be termed the "Mental Science" school holds that God is not a being of Love and Wisdom, but a force of attraction. They reject the Loving Father proclaimed by Jesus Christ and hold that man is the highest form of self-consciousness in the universe. In their philosophy, the human race is now at its highest point of understanding, and they see no evidence of there ever having been a "fall," or lapse in human evolution, thus rejecting a fundamental teaching of Christianity. If there has been no lapse in human evolution, there is no need for a mediator or a mental mender of broken laws; therefore, Jesus Christ and the entire Christian plan of redemption through him are eliminated from their philosophy.

There is another class of New Thought people who accept Christianity in its true sense and try to live according to the teachings of Jesus Christ. They perceive that

# TALKS ON TRUTH

There is a great truth hidden in the Christian religion that its orthodox followers have not discovered. With a scientific understanding of the mind and its laws of action, they have a key to the <term>occult</term> <note>hidden or inner</note> side of Christianity—and, in fact, all religions—that gives them an entirely new comprehension of life and its purpose. With this key, they unlock all the mysteries of religion and science, and place man where he belongs—as the master of all creation.

There are a number of schools, going by the general name of New Thought, that adhere to this Christian interpretation, yet even among them, there are minor differences. Each teacher colors the philosophy he promotes with his own mental bias. No one seems yet to have reached that place where the revelation from the <term>Spirit of Truth</term>, promised by Jesus to his followers, is entirely clear. For this reason, followers are never safe in accepting the teachings of any school as final, or as containing the pure truth. “Test all things; hold fast to that which is good,” is excellent advice.

This diversity we are referring to is not in fundamental principles—they are universally the same—but in how they are expressed and man’s relationship to them. For instance, there is a diversity of opinion regarding what is popularly known as the <term>“fall of man.”</term> There are those who proclaim that man, being spiritual, could never have fallen, while others accept the Christian doctrine of the “fall” and the redemption through Jesus Christ. In strict logic, man, being spiritual, could never be anything

From a study of the previous lessons, you should be convinced that man and the universe are under the direct creative power of a Supreme Being—call it what you will; and that man only needs to conform to its laws to be healthy, happy, and wise. It logically follows that all healing methods, whether applied to yourself or others, consist of creating a unity between the individual and the Universal Consciousness. No person heals themselves or another; the Supreme Mind does the work. “It is not I, but the Father within me, who does the works,” said Jesus. This is the testimony of all who are truly wise.

Then the first move in all healing is a recognition on your part, and the part of your patient, that God is present as an All-Powerful Mind, capable of healing every disease, no matter how bad it may appear. “With God all things are possible.” The best way to create this unity with the Father Mind is through prayer. “God is Spirit,” and he has a kingdom or ruling center in every soul. Do not look up or out for God, but “pray to the Father in secret”—silently within your own soul—“and he will reward you openly.” Many healers use the Lord’s Prayer at the beginning of every treatment. Talk to the Father as if he were a present identity. He is visible to your soul, and when you have attained that

### NERVOUSNESS

The mind sends its messages along the nerves. Say silently, “I am not subject to any kind of nervousness. My nerves are now harmonized, peaceful, and poised in Spirit and in Truth.”

### SIX DAYS’ TREATMENT

<header>TALKS ON TRUTH</header>

every thought and word is expressed in harmony with Truth.

The seventh day is a peaceful confidence and rest in the fulfillment of the Divine Law.

Through the use of these denials and affirmations for one week, a new and more orderly basis of thought is established in the mind, and the whole person is harmonized and vitalized. This process often heals stubborn cases, and the six-day course is recommended in conjunction with the special treatments.

Make your “denials” as if you were gently sweeping away cobwebs, and your “affirmations” with a strong, bold, vehement, and positive mind.

Each day’s treatment, and the whole course if necessary, is to be repeated over and over until it manifests its living presence and power in consciousness.

If you desire to help another who will not try, or cannot successfully bring their mind into harmonious relations with this Principle, think of him or her when you hold the daily thought, and the Spirit will cause your word to manifest both in you and in your patient.

> I acknowledge your presence and power, O Blessed Spirit, and in your Divine Wisdom now erase my mortal limitations, and from your pure substance of Love bring into manifestation my world according to your perfect law.

<header>HOW MENTAL HEALING IS DONE</header>

## MONDAY

[DENY]—I am no longer foolish or ignorant, and the foolishness and ignorance of my ancestors can no longer be visited upon me.

I am free from the foolishness and ignorance of the human race and of those with whom I associate. The foolishness and ignorance which may have been stored up by my own understanding are now erased.

[AFFIRM]—I am wise with the wisdom of Infinite Mind, and I have knowledge of all things. I know that I am pure intelligence, and I hereby claim my Divine right to light, life, and liberty in all goodness, wisdom, love, and purity. Let the light of Wisdom appear, and let the ignorance of human thought vanish.

## TUESDAY

[DENY]—I deny the belief that I have inherited disease, sickness, ignorance, or any mental limitation whatsoever. I deny all belief in evil, for God made all that really is and pronounced it good. Therefore, no such deception as a belief in evil can darken my clear understanding of Truth. Those with whom I associate can no longer deceive me with their words of consideration and sympathy. I can no longer deceive myself with such weakness.

Let these silly beliefs of darkened ignorance perish from my world. I am now free from them all, and by my powerful word, I hereby destroy them completely.

[AFFIRM]—God’s life is my life, and I vibrate with

### WEDNESDAY

[DENY]—I deny the belief that I am a child of the flesh and must suffer for the sins of my ancestors "even to the third and fourth generation." May all such ignorant claims vanish.

I deny that I inherited lustful passions and sensual appetites from my ancestors.

I deny the belief that the human race can project lustful passions and sensual appetites onto me. I deny the belief that those with whom I associate can project lustful passions or sensual appetites onto me. I deny my own ignorant belief in such false ideas.

[AFFIRM]—God is Spirit, and I—the Divine Image—am Spirit. I am born of God. God is too pure to look upon evil, and I am, therefore, Pure Being, without a trace of lust or passion.

### THURSDAY

[DENY]—I deny that the sins and failures of my ancestors can affect me in any way. Selfishness, envy, malice, jealousy, pride, greed, arrogance, cruelty, hypocrisy, stubbornness, and revenge are no part of my present understanding, and I deny all such beliefs in the human race, in those with whom I associate, and in my own mind.

### FRIDAY

[DENY]—I deny that I have inherited the consequences of fear from my ancestors, or that the human race can reflect its fears upon me. The fears of those with whom I associate can no longer hold me in sickness or want, and my own understanding is now fully rid of this illusion.

There is not and cannot hereafter be any fear in or about my body world.

[AFFIRM]—I am brave and bold with the knowledge that I am Spirit and all, and therefore not subject to any opposing power.

Plenty and prosperity are mine by inheritance from God, and I now, by my steady, persistent word, bring them into manifestation.

### SATURDAY

[DENY]—I deny that I inherit any belief that in any way limits me in health, virtue, intelligence, or power to do good.

I am one with and now fully manifest Infinite goodness and mercy.
